    #117 Spec: a red run with nobody at the other end keeps an issue, and the data repository checks itself (#65, folds #80)

    from #65real use #125mock-green

    /data/ promises "Every source is re-read daily — last run <date>". From 2026-10-08 to 2026-10-17 every scheduled and dispatched deploy failed at Build and check the dataset package, and the live page kept saying "last run 2026-10-07" for ten days. Nobody was told. The watch had run every morning; the deploy had not landed once.

    Problem Statement

    /data/ promises "Every source is re-read daily — last run <date>". From 2026-10-08 to 2026-10-17 every scheduled and dispatched deploy failed at Build and check the dataset package, and the live page kept saying "last run 2026-10-07" for ten days. Nobody was told. The watch had run every morning; the deploy had not landed once.

    Two things were missing:

    • A red deploy reaches no one. The watch's red has a kept issue (watch run failed, and since #62 overdue flags); the deploy has none. GitHub's own failure emails went out all ten days and changed nothing.
    • The data repository checks nothing. Its only workflow is the watch. The watch re-baselined an entry on 10-09 and turned a data test red (s8), and the first place that red showed was the site's deploy, one repository away. The watch pushes with the workflow token, so even a push-triggered check would not have started on its commits.

    And what the morning tells the tracker (#80, folded in) is decided in bash inside the watch workflow, where no test reaches it: which issue to create, comment on, reopen or close, a flag's title read back out of its own markdown, and two copies of the log printer.

    Solution

    Every run with nobody at the other end keeps one issue that says its own state:

    • the deploy keeps `deploy run failed` in the site's tracker;
    • the data repository's new check keeps `check failed on master` in the data tracker;
    • the watch keeps watch run failed and overdue flags as before, and watch run failed now closes itself on a green morning too.

    A red run of that kind opens or reopens its issue and comments with what went red and a link to the run. The first green run of the same kind closes it with a line naming that run. A person reads a kept issue and never has to close it.

    One module in the data package decides, for any kept issue, what the run does to it; one CLI carries that decision to the tracker without a shell. The workflows hand it the issue and the run's finding, and decide nothing else.

    The data repository runs its whole check on every push to master, every pull request, and after every watch run, so a test the watch's state breaks is red where it broke.

    User Stories

    1. As the human, I want a red scheduled deploy to open an issue in the site's tracker, so that ten silent days cannot happen again.
    2. As the human, I want a red dispatched deploy (the watch's "new state") to keep the same issue, so that the fast path is held to the same promise as the daily floor.
    3. As the human, I want a second red deploy to comment on the open issue rather than open another, so that one issue holds the whole story.
    4. As the human, I want a red deploy to reopen a closed deploy run failed, so that the issue's history stays in one place.
    5. As the human, I want the issue's body to name which jobs went red (build, deploy, pin, audit), so that I know where to look without opening the run.
    6. As the human, I want the body to say in one line whether today's page was published, so that I can tell a stale /data/ from an open advisory at a glance.
    7. As the human, I want the body to link the run, so that the evidence is one click away.
    8. As the human, I want the first green scheduled or dispatched deploy to close the issue with a line linking that run, so that an open issue always means "still red".
    9. As the human, I want a green push deploy to leave the issue alone, so that a build against the old pinned data never claims the daily page is current again.
    10. As the human, I want a red push deploy or a red hand-dispatched deploy to file nothing, so that the issue speaks only for runs nobody was watching.
    11. As a session merging a PR, I want the next morning's scheduled deploy to catch a red master I missed, so that a push red is never silent for more than a day.
    12. As the human, I want an IndexNow failure never to open the issue, so that a notification that cannot fail the deploy cannot raise the alarm either.
    13. As the human, I want the deploy's report to work when the data package fails to build, so that the issue opens in exactly the 10-08 case.
    14. As the human, I want deploy run failed under its own deploy label, created by the run when missing, so that it never reads as a slice or a kind.
    15. As the data repository, I want my whole check to run on every push to master, so that a merge that breaks the suite is red here.
    16. As a contributor, I want my pull request to the data repository checked, so that I see a red before anyone merges it.
    17. As the human, I want the check to run after every watch run, on master's head, so that the watch's own commits are checked even though they cannot trigger a push workflow.
    18. As the human, I want the check to run after a watch run that committed nothing, so that a test the calendar turns red is caught on its first morning.
    19. As the human, I want a red check after the watch to keep check failed on master in the data tracker, so that the 10-09 red would have reached me on 10-09.
    20. As the human, I want the first green check after the watch to close check failed on master, so that the issue means "master is red now".
    21. As the human, I want a red pull-request check or a red push check to file nothing, so that the issue speaks only for the run nobody is at.
    22. As the human, I want check failed on master's body to name the commit it checked and link the run, so that I know which state of master failed.
    23. As the human, I want watch run failed to close itself on a morning where everything but the overdue clock passed, so that data#31 does not collect comments after the outage ends.
    24. As the human, I want an overdue flag alone never to keep watch run failed open, so that overdue flags speaks for it alone, as today.
    25. As the human, I want a measurement run of the watch neither to open nor to close watch run failed, so that a run that leaves nothing behind still leaves nothing behind.
    26. As the human, I want overdue flags kept exactly as today (comment and reopen when overdue, close when clear, untouched when the clock is unread), so that the fold changes how it is decided, not what it does.
    27. As a maintainer, I want one table of finding × issue state → action, tested through one function, so that every kept issue behaves the same way.
    28. As a maintainer, I want a tracker answer the module cannot read to be its own action, so that a broken read is a red step and never a silent "nothing".
    29. As a maintainer, I want the issue found by its exact title, open or closed, so that a search matching words never picks the wrong issue.
    30. As a maintainer, I want the action turned into exact gh argument lists by a tested function, so that the commands the run sends are pinned by a test.
    31. As a maintainer, I want the CLI to run gh without a shell, so that a title or a body is never parsed as a command.
    32. As a maintainer, I want the tracker read done by the CLI through the same adapter, so that no gh issue list or jq decision is left in bash.
    33. As a maintainer, I want a flag's issue title to come from its FlagDocument, so that nothing parses it back out of the markdown.
    34. As a maintainer, I want this run's new flags handed from the watch's CLI to the filing step as a list, so that git status, head and sed stop deciding which issues are filed.
    35. As the human, I want flag issues still filed only after the state is pushed, so that nothing the watch read depends on the tracker answering.
    36. As a maintainer, I want one log printer shared by every CLI, so that a log line has one shape.
    37. As a maintainer, I want every step that touches a tracker to be one CLI command, so that the workflows hold no decision a test cannot reach.
    38. As a maintainer, I want the YAML tests to hold step order and each tracker step's if:, so that the decisions that live only in YAML (which runs keep an issue) are pinned, and a step's script is not.
    39. As the human, I want only the report jobs to hold issues: write, so that the jobs that build and test hold no write token.
    40. As the human, I want the check job read-only, its actions pinned to full SHAs, and no pull_request_target, so that a stranger's pull request cannot write anywhere.
    41. As a reader of threats.md, I want a row for "a red run with nobody at the other end", marked Checked with the tests that hold it, so that the claim names its proof.
    42. As the human, I want the deploy's words (which jobs, whether published) to live in the site and the data repository's words in the data repository, so that each run owns what it says.

    Implementation Decisions

    Numbered as the grill settled them (#65's decisions comment), so tickets can carry them.

    1. One module decides every kept issue. overdue flags, watch run failed, deploy run failed and check failed on master all go through one table: what the run found × the issue's state → the action. Each issue's own words (title, label, body) stay with the run that keeps it.
    2. The module lives in the data package, exported as an entry of its own beside the watch's run module. The deploy's report reads it from the data commit data.lock pins: that commit has built and deployed once, so a broken data master cannot silence the report. The data half lands first; the site half follows once a deploy has pinned a data commit that holds the module.
    3. Which deploy runs keep the issue: only schedule and repository_dispatch. A push and a workflow_dispatch have a person at the other end.
    4. Which reds count: any of build, deploy, pin, audit. IndexNow is continue-on-error and never reddens the run. The body names the red jobs and says in one line whether today's page was published.
    5. Which green closes it: only a green schedule or repository_dispatch run, with a closing line linking it. A push builds against the pinned data and proves nothing about the daily page.
    6. The deploy's issue: title deploy run failed, under a new red deploy label ("The daily deploy itself"), created by the run when missing.
    7. Where it lives: each run's kept issue lives in its own repository's tracker. No cross-repository token.
    8. The data check's triggers: push to master, pull_request, and workflow_run after source-watch completes (any conclusion). Each checks out master's head, or the pull request's head.
    9. The data check's kept issue: only workflow_run reds and greens keep check failed on master, under a new check label ("The data repository's own suite"). Its body links the run and names the master commit it checked.
    10. `watch run failed` closes itself on a non-measurement run where every step but the overdue clock passed, with a line linking it. A measurement run neither opens nor closes it.
    11. The seam. The glossary's Kept issue. The module's interface, settled in the grill:
        /** What the run found, in the issue's terms. */
    type RunFinding =
      | { kind: "red"; body: string }        // comment, or create, or reopen + comment
      | { kind: "green"; closing: string }   // close an open issue with this line
      | { kind: "unsaid" };                  // leave the issue as it is (the clock unread)

    /** The issue a run keeps, as written in its workflow. */
    interface KeptIssue { title: string; label: string }

    type KeptIssueAction =
      | { kind: "create"; title: string; label: string; body: string }
      | { kind: "comment"; number: number; reopen: boolean; body: string }
      | { kind: "close"; number: number; comment: string }
      | { kind: "nothing" }
      | { kind: "unread"; reason: string };

    /** `listed` is the tracker's answer as text (number, state and title of each issue under the label). */
    function actionOnKeptIssue(issue: KeptIssue, finding: RunFinding, listed: string): KeptIssueAction

    The issue is found by its exact title, open or closed. unread is the run's step failing, never a silent "nothing".

    1. Who runs `gh`: one CLI reads the tracker, decides through actionOnKeptIssue, turns the action into exact gh argument lists through a pure function (ghArgumentsOf(action)), and runs them with no shell. The workflows hand it the issue and the finding.
    2. gh is found from PATH's absolute folders only, never from the folder under check (Security: an outside program).
    3. The tracker read asks for a fixed limit, and an answer at or over it is unread; the flags list is bounded the same way (Security: every loop over outside input is bounded).
    4. The deploy's `report` job: the last job of the deploy workflow. It needs build, deploy, pin and audit, runs whatever their result on schedule and repository_dispatch only, and is the only job holding issues: write. A site module, deployFindingOf(jobResults, runUrl), turns the jobs' results into the finding and its words; the job hands that finding to the data package's CLI built from the data.lock commit.
    5. Job results the module cannot read (malformed, a job missing, an unknown result) give a red finding that says the report could not read the jobs' results, never green (Security: data crossing a boundary is validated).
    6. Flag issues: FlagDocument carries its title. The watch's CLI writes this run's new flags (file and title) to a list outside the repository, in the runner's temporary directory. After the persist step has pushed, one CLI files one source-change issue per flag on that list through the same adapter, the body read from the flag file. The git status, head, sed and path-guard bash goes.
    7. One printer, one command per tracker step. One print(LogEvent) in a small file of its own, used by every CLI; the two copies go. Every step that touches a tracker is one CLI command, the clock's read of the open flags included: the clock's CLI reads through the adapter, turns readClock's reading into a finding (findingOfClock), keeps overdue flags, and still exits red on overdue.
    8. The data check workflow: a check job with contents: read only, on ubuntu-26.04 (named, so the browser cases drive the image's own Chrome), running npm ci and npm run check; a report job after it with issues: write, on workflow_run only. No pull_request_target. The actions and their full SHAs are the watch's.
    9. The bump: Z, bug.
    10. Real use: (1) the first scheduled morning after the data half's merge: the check runs by workflow_run and is green, and data#31 closes itself if every source reads, or gains a comment and stays if an outage holds; (2) after the site half's merge, on the human's word at that moment, one repository_dispatch carrying a sha that is not a data commit opens deploy run failed, and the next 06:40 scheduled deploy closes it; (3) the human says whether that issue reached their own device before the session pointed at it, and whether its body told them which job went red and whether the page was published. The check's red path is proven before the PR, not in real use: a red data master on purpose would redden the live deploy.
    11. Out of scope: see below.

    Also:

    • The site's threat model gains a row, "a red run with nobody at the other end", Checked by the kept issues and the tests below.
    • The deploy and watch workflows' header comments that describe the issues they file are corrected where this change makes them untrue.

    Testing Decisions

    A good test asserts a decision through a public interface: the action for a finding and a tracker state, the argument lists for an action, the finding for a set of job results. It never asserts the wording of a body beyond what the decision requires (which jobs are named, whether "published" is said, that the run is linked).

    The seams, agreed before the spec:

    Data repository

    1. actionOnKeptIssue: every cell of findings (red, green, unsaid) × the tracker's answer (no such issue, open, closed, unreadable), the exact-title match against a near-miss title, and an unreadable answer giving unread.
    2. ghArgumentsOf: reopen before comment, the label created before an issue is created, a title or body passed as one argument.
    3. findingOfClock: clear → green, overdue → red with its body, unread → unsaid.
    4. sayRun (existing): every FlagDocument carries its title.
    5. The kept-issue CLI and the flag-filing CLI as child processes, with a stub gh first on PATH that answers the tracker read and records every argument list: one case per action, and a hostile title ($(…), quotes, a backtick) that reaches gh intact. This is where the red path is proven before the PR. Prior art: the site's carry tests' entryRunAsChildProcess.
    6. The workflow YAML tests (existing workflow-text helpers, rebuild-dispatch.test.ts): step order, each tracker step's if: (measurement runs keep nothing; check failed on master only on workflow_run), and each job's permissions. Nothing about a step's script.

    Site repository

    1. deployFindingOf: each red job named, published or not by the deploy job's result, green only when all four passed or were skipped by design, the run linked.
    2. tests/pipeline.test.ts (existing): report needs the four jobs, runs whatever their result on schedule and repository_dispatch only, and is the only job with issues: write.

    The gh adapter inside the CLI is covered by seam 5 only.

    Out of Scope

    • The Road showing kept issues from both repositories (declined in the grill; no idea opened).
    • The outages behind data#31 (sources unreachable on 10-18 to 10-20). This slice only lets that issue close itself.
    • "No test pins a field the watch rewrites" (retro of #12, on #97). The data check now makes such a test red where it breaks; the rule stays a retro candidate.
    • GitHub's own failure emails, left as they are.
    • Spine's road-redraw hook redrawing on another repository's issue close (on #97).

    Further Notes

    • Folds in #80 (closed into #65 at the re-plan of 2026-10-18) and the retro of #49's requirement (#65's comment of 2026-10-19).
    • Data issues named in full: OytunOnal/permit-rulebook-data#31 (watch run failed, open since 10-18), OytunOnal/permit-rulebook-data#28 (the s8 fix that ended the ten days).
    • The workflow token cannot start a workflow from a push it made; workflow_run is how the watch's commits are checked without giving the watch a new permission.

    Tickets

    • #118 Kept issue: overdue flags is kept through one module and one CLI, end to end (data repository)
    • #119 Kept issue: watch run failed is kept through the CLI and closes itself on a green morning (data repository)
    • #120 Flag issues are filed from FlagDocument.title, through the kept-issue adapter, after the push (data repository)
    • #121 The data repository checks itself on push, pull request and after every watch run, and keeps check failed on master (data repository)
    • #122 A red scheduled or dispatched deploy keeps deploy run failed (site repository)

    Comments

    OytunOnal · 20 Oct 2026

    Ruling on Testing Decisions, seam 5 (the human, 2026-10-20, before #118's brief).

    A stub gh cannot sit first on PATH on Windows without a shell: Node refuses to spawn a .cmd or .bat without shell: true, and the rule is no shell. So seam 5 is built as three parts, for the kept-issue CLI (#118) and the flag-filing CLI (#120) alike:

    1. The CLI as a function. Its entry is a function taking the arguments and a runner (gh's argument list → its answer). One in-process case per action, and the hostile title, record exactly what reaches the runner.
    2. The resolver. A pure function finds gh in PATH's absolute folders only, never a relative one or the folder under check (gh on Linux, gh.exe on Windows), tested with temporary folders.
    3. One real child-process case. The CLI with a stub gh shell script first on PATH, proving the real execFile with no shell. It runs on Linux, so CI and #121's check run it; a local Windows run skips it by name, and it refuses to skip when CI is set (the browser cases' "CI cannot skip" precedent).

    OytunOnal · 20 Oct 2026

    #118's open choices, accepted by the human (2026-10-20), before its merger:

    1. The clock's step stays green on overdue; the run reddens at "Fail the run on an overdue flag", as today. A red step would trip failure() and file watch run failed for an overdue flag alone (user story 24). Decision 15's "still exits red on overdue" is held by the run.
    2. ghArgumentsOf(action, labelLook) takes LabelLook {color, description}, so creating a label never repaints an existing one.
    3. findingOfClock(reading, runUrl) takes the run's URL, so the finding links the run.
    4. TRACKER_READ_LIMIT = 100; the tracker is read by label alone, --state all, no --search; the module matches the exact title.
    5. OPEN_FLAGS_READ_LIMIT = 1000; an answer that long is unread.
    6. An unreadable tracker answer is unread for every finding, unsaid included.
    7. When an open and a closed issue share a title, the open one is the issue.
    8. A finding file is refused if it is not a regular file (a link included), over 256 KiB, or not a valid finding.
    9. Layout: kept-issue.ts (exported as permit-rulebook-data/kept-issue), keepIssue(argv, runGh) in keep-issue.ts, ghExecutableOf and ghRunnerAt in gh.ts, the entry cli-kept-issue.ts, the printer print.ts.
    10. "The folder under check" is the working directory: gh is never taken from it or from anything inside it.

    The kept-issue CLI's arguments: --title, --label, --label-color, --label-description, then --finding red --body … | --finding green --closing … | --finding unsaid | --finding-file <json>.

    OytunOnal · 20 Oct 2026

    #119's open choices, accepted by the human (2026-10-20), before its merger:

    1. The finding comes from ${{ job.status == 'success' && 'green' || 'red' }} (failure() is not available in a step's env); any status but success is red.
    2. CLOSING: "Closed by a morning on which every step but the overdue clock passed. Run: <run url>".
    3. BODY is today's body word for word, in the step's environment.
    4. findingOn(step, jobStatus), a test helper, returns the finding the step's expression gives for a job status.

    Not done, carried to the review: CONTRIBUTING.md's lines on watch run failed do not say the issue now closes itself. job.status mid-job is GitHub's documented behaviour, first seen on the first scheduled morning (real use, step 1).

    OytunOnal · 20 Oct 2026

    Ruling (the human, 2026-10-20), from #121's report: a broken master build must not silence a kept issue.

    The kept-issue CLI existed only after the repository's whole npm run build, so a master that stops compiling left watch run failed (#119's step) and check failed on master (#121's report job) with no CLI to run; the bash #119 replaced reported whatever the build did.

    (a), chosen: the CLI is built on its own, first: a small TypeScript config naming only the CLI and what it imports (the module, the gh adapter, the printer, the entry), built into its own folder before the repository's build, in the watch job and in the check's report job; the reporting steps run that copy. A broken file elsewhere cannot silence a report; a break in the CLI's own files is caught by every pull request's check before it merges.

    Carried in the fix round, with the review's findings.

    OytunOnal · 20 Oct 2026

    #121's open choices, accepted by the human (2026-10-20), before its merger:

    1. workflow_run fires only for watch runs on master (branches: [master]).
    2. The check job checks out the pull request's head on a pull request and master's head otherwise, with persist-credentials: false.
    3. The report job runs always() && github.event_name == 'workflow_run'.
    4. The finding: needs.check.result == 'success' && 'green' || 'red'.
    5. The commit named in the body: github.sha.
    6. The check label's colour: b60205.
    7. The report job installs with npm ci --ignore-scripts.
    8. Body: "The data check on master went red after the watch: the check job ended <result> on commit <sha>. Run: <url>"; closing: "The data check on master is green again on commit <sha>. Run: <url>".
    9. GH_REPO: ${{ github.repository }}.

    Red path proven on GitHub's runner: https://github.com/OytunOnal/permit-rulebook-data/actions/runs/39431006228 (check red on the deliberate case only, report skipped; seam 5's child-process case passed on Linux, 22/22).

    OytunOnal · 20 Oct 2026

    #120's open choices, accepted by the human on 2026-10-20 (branch kept-issues-120, built at e58b9d6):

    1. cli-watch takes --new-flags=<path>; the workflow test holds that the watch and the filing step name the same $RUNNER_TEMP/new-flags.json.
    2. newFlagsListOf(flags) returns the list as {file, title} and lives in flag-issues.ts, so the writer and the reader share one shape.
    3. The list is written after the state: a failed write costs the run its flag issues, never its state.
    4. NEW_FLAGS_LIMIT is 100 (the watchlist holds 46 entries); a longer list is refused whole.
    5. Size caps: 256 KiB for the list, 64 KiB for each flag file.
    6. FLAG_FILE_NAME is a plain .md name directly in the flags folder; a title is one line and not empty; links and folders are refused.
    7. Each flag runs label create --force with the same colour and description.
    8. The label's words come from the workflow as arguments; cli-overdue still hard-codes source-change.
    9. The filing step's if: is ${{ success() && github.event.inputs.commit != 'false' }}, so a failed push files nothing.
    10. The step keeps its name, "Open issues for new flags only", and the workflow's comments were rewritten to match.

    Sent back before the merge: the list held every flag the run wrote, so a second committing run on the same day (a hand dispatch with commit=true after the scheduled run) would file a second issue for a due reminder, where the old step filed none. The list will hold only the flags whose file this run created or changed, red first, with the green rerun.

    Ruling on PR OytunOnal/permit-rulebook-data#36's review (Spec 5), 2026-10-20, amending decision 10:

    Only a run on the default branch keeps watch run failed. The watch's "Say so when the run fails" step gains a check that the run's ref is the default branch, beside !cancelled() and commit != 'false', and a workflow test holds it. A hand dispatch on another branch (a baseline run, dispatch=false) neither opens nor closes the issue: its green would say the morning's run had recovered, and its red would raise the alarm for a run nobody waits on. Carried by the fix round.

    The check's report job has written to the tracker only in-process. No red is forced on master to see it live: a broken master commit would reach the site's data pin. Its first live write is the first real red check on master. The real-use issue names that event: read the first check failed on master the job opens, then its closing comment on the next green.

    PR OytunOnal/permit-rulebook-data#36's fix round (aecf428..f292022), its names and open choices accepted by the human on 2026-10-20, with one rename: src/watch/cli-shared.ts becomes src/watch/cli-io.ts, since it holds how a CLI reads its arguments and files and finds gh.

    Names: kept-issue-command.ts (was keep-issue.ts) and its test; overdue-command.ts (readClockOnFlags, overdueBodyOf, NAMED_OVERDUE_LIMIT); cli-io.ts (readArguments, readBoundedFile, messageOf, ghRunnerFromPath); failedRun, comparablePathOf, keptIssueArgs, filingArgsOf; tsconfig.kept-issue.json, build:kept-issue, KEPT_ISSUE_CLI_COPY; tests/stub-gh.ts; watch.yml's FLAG_LABEL… and WATCH_LABEL… env.

    Choices:

    1. The watch builds the kept-issue CLI on its own right after npm ci, into dist/kept-issue-cli/, and both kept-issue steps (watch run failed, overdue flags) run that copy; the check's report job builds only the copy, never npm run build; npm run check builds it too, and the child-process cases run it.
    2. Only a run on master keeps watch run failed: github.ref == 'refs/heads/master', the dispatch step's form.
    3. The overdue body names at most 50 flags, oldest first, and one line counts the rest.
    4. The clock takes only the label's name; it reads under it and creates nothing.
    5. A missing flag file means this run creates the flag; a link, a folder or a file over 64 KiB is an error.
    6. ghRunnerFromPath returns the runner or the "not found" line, which the CLI prints before exiting 1 having run nothing; the clock exits 1 when it cannot write its finding file or step output.

    Not red first, accepted: the two CLIs moved onto the one printer have no case (no agreed seam reaches them; their readers take only the exit code); the four-clause condition is gone into the shared parser's required arguments rather than named.

    #122's open choices, accepted by the human on 2026-10-20 (branch deploy-report, built at f1eb3b0):

    1. scripts/deploy-finding.mjs (@level domain) holds deployFindingOf(jobResults, runUrl) and its words; plain .mjs with a JSDoc type import of RunFinding from permit-rulebook-data/kept-issue, so the report job runs it with no site install.
    2. The body's and closing line's words live in that module as DEPLOY_FINDING_WORDS, not in src/lib/copy.ts: copy.ts holds the visitor's words, and an issue's reader is the maintainer (the data side keeps its kept issues' words in its workflow).
    3. The red body: a lead; "Jobs that did not pass: name (result), …"; "Today's page was published." or "… was not published."; the run's link.
    4. The closing line: "Closed by a green scheduled or dispatched deploy. Run: <url>".
    5. Passed is success, or skipped for pin only (SKIPPED_BY_DESIGN); a deploy skipped behind a red build is named as "deploy (skipped)".
    6. Unreadable, and so red: a result other than GitHub's four (JOB_RESULTS), text over 1024 characters (JOB_RESULTS_LIMIT), not JSON, not an object, a job missing.
    7. JOB_RESULTS is one JSON object of the four needs.<job>.result values assembled in the workflow, not toJSON(needs), which would carry the build's URL list toward Linux's limit on one environment string.
    8. scripts/cli-deploy-finding.mjs (@level main) reads JOB_RESULTS and RUN_URL and prints the finding; the step writes it to $RUNNER_TEMP/deploy-finding.json, which the kept-issue CLI reads with --finding-file.
    9. The report job checks out the site to site/ and the data to data-at-lock/, both with persist-credentials: false; a step checks that data.lock's sha is 40 hex before it becomes the data checkout's ref:; npm ci --ignore-scripts, then only npm run build:kept-issue; it runs data-at-lock/dist/kept-issue-cli/src/watch/cli-kept-issue.js.
    10. Its permissions are contents: read and issues: write, as the data check's report job.
    11. The label is deploy, colour b60205, "The daily deploy itself"; title, label and colour sit in the workflow step.
    12. The if: is exactly ${{ always() && (github.event_name == 'schedule' || github.event_name == 'repository_dispatch') }}, and the test holds that string.
    13. pages.yml's header, permissions floor, build and audit comments are made true.
    14. The threat row "A red run with nobody at the other end" is Checked, naming tests/deploy-finding.test.ts, the tests/pipeline.test.ts describe, and the data side's kept-issue.test.ts, kept-issue-command.test.ts, overdue-command.test.ts and check-workflow.test.ts.

    Also accepted: tests/audit-and-runner.test.ts, outside the two seams, skips a job whose if: starts with always() when it checks that no job waits on the audit; the report waits on the audit only to say its red, and runs whatever its result.

    OytunOnal · 20 Oct 2026

    Ruling on PR #124's review (Spec 2), 2026-10-20, amending #122's accepted choice 12:

    The deploy's report job runs only on a run's first attempt, as pin and indexnow do: its if: is always(), a schedule or repository_dispatch event, and github.run_attempt == 1. A re-run builds against data.lock's old data, so its green would close deploy run failed while /data/ stays stale (decision 5), and a re-run has a person at the other end (decision 3). The test holds the new string. Carried by the fix round.

    OytunOnal · 20 Oct 2026

    Ruling on PR #124's review (Standards 10), 2026-10-20, correcting #122's accepted choice 8:

    scripts/cli-deploy-finding.mjs is // @level presentation, not main. It reads the CLI's input from the environment, hands it to deployFindingOf (domain) and prints the answer: a CLI handler. It builds no adapter and wires nothing, and scripts/ is no deep module, so main does not fit. A file's level comes from its job under the levels' definitions. structure.json is regenerated in the fix round.

    OytunOnal · 20 Oct 2026

    PR #124's fix round (b970f13..9b8eda2), its names and open choices accepted by the human on 2026-10-20:

    Names: hasPassed, jobsThatDidNotPass, jobsAndResultsText, jobWithNoKnownResult, isJsonObject, GITHUB_JOB_RESULT_WORDS; readJobResults returns a reading tagged { kind: "read", results } or { kind: "unread", reason }; DEPLOY_FINDING_WORDS.unread and .unreadReasons (tooLong, notJson, notAnObject, noKnownResult(job), noRunUrl); redFindingOf, unreadLineOf; jobKeyValueOf(job, key) in tests/workflow-text.ts.

    Choices:

    1. The unread lead reads "The report could not read what the run found, so it cannot say the deploy is green: <reason>", since an empty run address is not a job's result; each reason names what failed.
    2. An empty run address (exactly "") gives red: the lead and its reason, with no "Run:" line.
    3. The 40-hex check of data.lock's sha stays as three case copies in pages.yml: a shared reader would be a new file reached through two checkout layouts with no test at the agreed seams, and a one-line grep -Eqx would let a multi-line payload through.
    4. The threat row and pages.yml's header and report comments say first attempts only, and that a run with no address is red.

    Noted: 730db73 broke the build on its own (an untyped parameter in the new case); 9b8eda2 fixes it on top, without rewriting the pushed history.

    #86 A fast second click answers the next question: no beat between questions

    real use #132real-green

    From the v1.2 gate critique (docs/spine/critiques/2026-10-18-v1.2-gate-critique.md, isolated full walk on PRODUCT_RUBRIC 1.3). Screenshots are in docs/spine/critiques/2026-10-18-v1.2-gate-critique/. Finding F4, half of recommended adjustment 3. Applied by the human on 2026-10-18.

    From the v1.2 gate critique (docs/spine/critiques/2026-10-18-v1.2-gate-critique.md, isolated full walk on PRODUCT_RUBRIC 1.3). Screenshots are in docs/spine/critiques/2026-10-18-v1.2-gate-critique/. Finding F4, half of recommended adjustment 3. Applied by the human on 2026-10-18.

    What a reader sees

    Every question's options sit in the same spot. On the hostile walk, two blind clicks landed on real answers to questions not yet read (offer country France; under 3 years). The declared panel shows the damage and the pencil repairs it, which is why this is friction and not a blocker. But nothing marks the change of question for a mouse user: no transition and no focus move. Screens: 39-hostile-q4-offer-country-by-accident.jpg, 40-hostile-q6-innovative-company.jpg.

    The proposed fix (adjustment 3)

    After an answer, focus moves to the new question's heading, not its first option, and the new card arrives with a short reveal timed from the tokens. A second click in the same spot then lands on nothing.

    Done when

    • A click repeated at the same point within the beat answers nothing, and a click after it answers.
    • The keyboard path reaches the first option in zero Tabs, as now (lens 7 scored 5/5 and must hold).
    • The guard holds under prefers-reduced-motion, where the fade is off, and the first paint still holds still (CLS 0 on every beat).

    Rewritten 2026-10-21 from the decisions comment below: focus stays on the first option, so the heading move and its one Tab are gone.

    Comments

    OytunOnal · 21 Oct 2026

    Decisions, 2026-10-21 (grilled before `/implement`)

    1. Guard: after a gesture draws a question card, its options take no click for one beat. The guard is separate from the motion and holds under prefers-reduced-motion. It sits where an option's click is handled, so a key's Enter is caught too. It covers the choice buttons (.opt) and the list's rows (.copt).
    2. Entrance: the card fades in, using opacity only, so no box moves. Reduced motion turns the fade off and keeps the guard.
    3. Focus stays on the new card's first option (F6's rule, focusFirst). It does not move to the heading: four questions (recognition_de, occupation_shortage, fr_innovative_employer, top200_grad) put a "Not sure?" link before the options, and a reader would need two Tabs.
    4. Timing: one token, --motion-beat: 300ms, under a new "Motion" heading in tokens.css. The fade and the guard both read it, the script once from the computed style. 300 ms catches a reflex double-click and lets through a deliberate click from someone who has read the new question.
    5. Scope: every question card a gesture draws gets a beat: an answer, a correction, ← Back, Keep this answer, Start over, and the browser's Back and Forward. The first paint and the verdict have none.
    6. Term: beat, now in GLOSSARY.md. Neither reveal (s20's scroll), arrival (a reader sent from a country or route page) nor entrance (s37) is used for it.
    7. Seam: the built interview page in a real browser (prior art: tests/history.test.ts, tests/arrival.test.ts, and the layout-shift observer in tests/first-paint.test.ts). The decisions it tests:
    8. two clicks at the same point within the beat leave one answer on the record;
    9. a click after the beat answers;
    10. two fast Enters on the focused first option leave one answer;
    11. after an answer, focus is on the new card's first option;
    12. under emulated prefers-reduced-motion, the guard holds and the card runs no animation;
    13. CLS reads 0 across an answer;
    14. on the first paint, an option answers at once.

    No ADR: this is easy to reverse, since the token and the guard are each one place. The "Done when" above is rewritten to match.

    #133 Spec: the German Blue Card's IT path, and a question says which routes ask it (#77, folds #88)

    from #77real use #146real-green

    Specced from #77 (folds #88 and the v1.2.1 gate's adjustment 3 "why we ask"), grilled 2026-10-21.

    Specced from #77 (folds #88 and the v1.2.1 gate's adjustment 3 "why we ask"), grilled 2026-10-21.

    Problem Statement

    A reader with an Indian passport and a German job offer answers "And in the last seven years?" with "3 to under 5 years", and no Germany card reads it: "Under 3" and "3 to under 5" give the same result on all eight German cards. The German Blue Card's own source, the ZAV page the dataset already quotes and the watch reads daily, has a special rule for IT specialists: no formal qualification, three years of relevant IT experience in the last seven, €45,934.20. Nothing models it, so an IT worker without a degree is never shown the Blue Card the law gives them, and the card's scope line ("every rule that decides … is a question you answer") is untrue.

    Around it, the interview asks questions without saying why, and keeps or drops answers without saying so:

    • "And in the last seven years?" leans on a question that is not always asked before it (France asks no five-year window).
    • A reader with a German job offer is asked for living-cost funds, which only the Opportunity Card reads, and nothing says so; on the "any of these four" path a reader who names Spain is asked two Dutch questions with no line saying which country asks.
    • A money question answered "Doesn't apply to me, or I don't know" shows "—" in the declared panel with no pencil: the one row that cannot be reopened.
    • After a change of country with the pencil, answers the new scope does not read are dropped in silence, while the edit line promises "the rest are kept".
    • The test that should catch an answer that changes nothing checks a field across all destinations at once, so Spain, the Netherlands and France made the seven-year question pass.

    Solution

    • The German shortage-occupation Blue Card gains its IT path: a reader in IT work with three or more years in the last seven, offered €45,934.20 or more in Germany, meets it without a degree. The two conditions the source sets that no reader can answer, an academic-level IT job and the Federal Employment Agency's approval, are quoted and listed with the card's rules, never asked.
    • The seven-year question stands on its own: "Related skilled work experience in the last seven years?".
    • A question that only some of the reader's open routes read carries an asked-for line naming them: "Asked for: Opportunity Card (Chancenkarte, § 20a) · Germany", or a count per country when there are more than two.
    • A money row answered "Doesn't apply to me, or I don't know" says so in the declared panel and has its pencil.
    • After a change of country, the answers set aside because no route in the new scope reads them are named once, in the declared panel; the edit line promises only what happens.
    • The asking rule's test checks every destination, and every pair of neighbouring rungs of a ladder question.

    User Stories

    1. As an IT worker without a degree with a German job offer, I want the Blue Card judged on its IT path, so that I see a permit the law gives me.
    2. As that reader, I want "3 to under 5 years" in the last seven to change my Blue Card result against "Under 3 years", so that the answer I gave is read.
    3. As that reader, I want the card to name the IT path as the one my result rests on, so that I know which rule I met.
    4. As that reader, I want a short IT path measured against the same €45,934.20 the card shows, so that the gap I see is the real one.
    5. As that reader, I want the academic-level IT job and the Federal Employment Agency's approval listed with the card's rules and quoted, so that I know what the authority still checks.
    6. As that reader, I want not to be asked whether my job is at academic level, so that the interview does not ask me a judgement I cannot make.
    7. As an IT worker without a degree who is unsure whether "web developer" is on the shortage list, I want my answer to the shortage question not to cost me the IT path, so that a question the limb never asks cannot fail me.
    8. As a reader with a recognised degree in a shortage occupation, I want the Blue Card judged exactly as before, so that the new path changes nothing for me.
    9. As a reader with a recognised degree who also works in IT, I want my result to rest on whichever path I meet, so that I am never shown short when one path holds.
    10. As a reader of the Blue Card's scope line, I want it to stay true, so that "every rule that decides is a question you answer" is a promise I can rely on.
    11. As a reader on France's path, I want the seven-year question to make sense without a five-year question before it, so that its words never point at something I was not asked.
    12. As a reader with a German job offer, I want the funds question to say it is asked for the Opportunity Card, so that I understand why a job-seeker's question reaches me.
    13. As that reader, I want the age question to say the same, so that every question I meet has a reason I can read.
    14. As a reader on Germany's path, I want the second experience window to name the routes that read it, so that I see why the window changed.
    15. As a reader on the "any of these four" path who named Spain, I want each Dutch question to say it is asked for a Dutch route, by name, so that I am not puzzled by another country's questions.
    16. As a reader, I want no asked-for line on a question every open route reads, such as my passport, so that the line appears only where it explains something.
    17. As a reader whose question feeds many routes in several countries, I want the line to count them per country, so that it stays one short line.
    18. As a reader, I want the routes in the line named exactly as their result cards name them, so that I can find them in my result.
    19. As a reader meeting "§" first in an asked-for line, I want it expanded there as it is everywhere on the page, so that the symbol is explained where I first see it.
    20. As a reader reopening a question with the pencil, I want its asked-for line to name the routes it is asked for as if I had not answered it, so that the line is true in edit mode too.
    21. As a reader on a phone, I want a long asked-for line to wrap cleanly, so that it never pushes the options off the card.
    22. As a reader who answered "Doesn't apply to me, or I don't know" to the funds question, I want the panel to show that answer, so that an answered question never looks unanswered.
    23. As that reader, I want that row's pencil, so that I can change the answer like any other.
    24. As a reader who answered "Doesn't apply…" to a salary question, I want the same, so that every money row behaves alike.
    25. As a reader who changes the offer country from France to Spain, I want to be told which of my answers were set aside because Spain's routes do not use them, so that nothing I said disappears in silence.
    26. As that reader, I want the set-aside line next to my answers, on whichever screen comes next, question or result, so that I see it where my answers are.
    27. As that reader, I want the line gone on the following screen, so that it never piles up or goes stale.
    28. As a reader editing an answer, I want the edit line to promise that the answers my new choice still uses are kept, so that it never claims more than happens.
    29. As a reader who changes back to France, I want the set-aside questions asked again, so that I never carry an answer I gave for a scope I left.
    30. As a reader, I want every answer in my declared panel read by at least one card, so that nothing I was asked is decoration.
    31. As a maintainer, I want a test that fails when a field can be asked on a destination whose screen no change of that answer moves, so that B1's class of bug cannot ship again.
    32. As a maintainer, I want a test that fails when two neighbouring rungs of a ladder question read the same on a destination where it is asked, so that "Under 3" and "3 to under 5" could never again give the same result unseen.
    33. As a maintainer, I want the asked-for routes to come from the engine's own asking reckoning, so that the line can never name a route that does not read the answer.
    34. As a maintainer, I want the IT path's sentences quoted from the watched page, so that the daily watch guards them like every other value.

    Implementation Decisions

    Data (the data package)

    1. The IT path is a second path on `de-blue-card-shortage`, not a route of its own. Outside the disjunction stay the passport, the destination, the offer, "located in Germany" and the salary threshold (€45,934.20, unchanged). Inside an any:
    2. recognised degree: qualification = degree, recognition_de = recognized, occupation_shortage = yes, as today;
    3. IT specialist: occupation_it = yes, experience_7y in {3to5, 5plus}. Each IT path criterion carries its source: the paragraph "Sonderregelung für IT-Fachkräfte" on https://www.arbeitsagentur.de/vor-ort/zav/working-and-living-in-germany/newsletter-iss/03-2026/blaue-karte, quoted, dated, legal_basis § 18g Abs. 2 AufenthG.
    4. `occupation_shortage` moves inside the degree path. The IT path does not read it: the limb's occupations (ISCO 133 and 25) are already on the shortage list the question's learn link cites.
    5. Two preconditions stated, never asked, quoted from the same paragraph, each worded as applying to the IT path ("Without a degree, in IT: …"): the job is an IT job at academic level; the Federal Employment Agency approves the employment. Both join the card's scope.not_asked, and the scope's reason names them.
    6. Nothing changes on `de-blue-card-general`. A reader without a degree earning above its threshold meets the shortage card's IT path. exclusions.md's § 19c IT row stays: it is a different rule.
    7. `experience_7y`'s label becomes "Related skilled work experience in the last seven years?". Its short label stays "Experience (last 7 years)".
    8. The engine exports the asking reckoning per question: for a profile and a field, the routes the question is asked for (the scored routes with no hard fail whose undecided criteria read the field, by the same walk the asking rule takes) and the open routes it was measured against. It decides nothing new: it names what the asking rule already computes. Its name is a verb that says what it returns.

    Site

    1. The asked-for line is drawn on a question card when the routes the question is asked for are fewer than the open routes.
    2. One or two routes: each by its card's own name and its country ("Asked for: Opportunity Card (Chancenkarte, § 20a) · Germany").
    3. More than two: a count per country ("Asked for: 3 routes in Germany, 1 in the Netherlands").
    4. Route names pass through the screen's one-time "§" expansion, like every route name on the page.
    5. In edit mode the reckoning runs on the profile without the field being edited.
    6. The line may wrap at 390 px; it never pushes the options off the card.
    7. Its words live in copy.ts; the route and country names come from the dataset.
    8. The money rows. A money field answered with the "unknown" band shows the field's unknown_label as written ("Doesn't apply to me, or I don't know") in the declared panel, with its pencil, and reopens like every row. This covers funds_eur_month, salary_eur_year and salary_eur_month.
    9. Set-aside answers. The record's replay after an edit reports the answers it set aside, in asked order. The declared panel draws a set-aside line naming them by their short labels, for one screen, whether the next screen is a question or the result; the next render takes it away (as the resumed line borrows the panel for one screen, s20). A later change back asks the set-aside questions again; nothing restores them.
    10. The edit line "Your answer — pick another to change it, the rest are kept" becomes a promise of only what happens ("answers your new choice still uses are kept"), and moves into copy.ts, where every reader-facing string lives.
    11. F2's choice is the line, not the branch: the funds and age questions stay asked on the offer path, because the Opportunity Card in scope reads them, and say so through the asked-for line.

    Order: the data change lands first, in the data repository; the site takes it through data.lock.

    Testing Decisions

    A good test here asserts a decision, never the wording: which routes the line names, whether a row has a pencil, which answers were set aside, which result a rung gives. No source-grep; the engine is run and the page is read.

    Seam 1, the data package's public engine (`evaluate`, `remainingQuestions` and the new reckoning) on the real dataset.

    • The IT path: met for IT work + 3to5 + €45,934.20 with no degree; short by the experience rung with lt3; its deciding path is the IT path; the shortage answer does not reach it; the degree path behaves as before; a reader meeting both is met.
    • "Under 3" and "3 to under 5" give different German results for an IT reader with no degree.
    • The two preconditions are in the card's scope, as not asked.
    • The reckoning names the Opportunity Card alone for the funds question on a Germany-offer profile, and every open route for citizenship.
    • questions-do-work gains two checks:
    • per destination: for every destination where a field can be asked, some change of its answer moves that destination's screen;
    • per rung: for every ladder question (an enum with kind: "improvable") on every destination where it is asked, each pair of neighbouring rungs moves that destination's screen for some profile. Money bands and non-ladder enums are outside the per-rung check: bands' edges are global today (#89 carries the per-country bands and extends the check to them), and a passport list gives one result for most countries by design.
    • Prior art: tests/chancenkarte.test.ts, tests/deciding-path.test.ts, tests/questions-do-work.test.ts in the data repository.

    Seam 2, the built interview page in a real browser, walked by answers (scripts/browser.mjs).

    • The asked-for line: present on funds and age on a Germany-offer walk, naming the Opportunity Card; absent on the passport question; naming the Dutch routes on the "any of these four" path with Spain; counting per country when more than two; its "§" expanded on first meeting; true in edit mode.
    • The money rows: "Doesn't apply…" on funds shows its answer and its pencil, and the pencil reopens the question.
    • The set-aside line: after France → Spain with an answer only France reads, the next screen names it, the one after does not; on a change that leads to the result, the result's panel names it.
    • The seven-year question's wording on France's path.
    • Each case asserts its precondition first (the card drawn, the row present) before reading its result.
    • Prior art: tests/s25.test.ts, tests/record.test.ts, tests/beat.test.ts.

    Every new case fails before its code.

    Structure

    Out of Scope

    • Money bands cut per destination, and the per-rung check over them: #89.
    • Asking whether an IT job is at academic level, or asking the Federal Employment Agency's approval: both stated, not asked (decision 3).
    • An IT limb on the general Blue Card: not needed (decision 4).
    • A short name per route for the asked-for line: the card's own name is used.
    • Restoring set-aside answers when a reader changes back.
    • The rest of the v1.2.1 gate's adjustment 3, which went to #56.

    Further Notes

    • Bump: bug, Z, v1.2.2. Not major, so no ADR.
    • The data repository's CONTRIBUTING.md is the data contract for decisions 1–5. A site fixture the new pin moves regenerates with its reason in its own source line.
    • GLOSSARY: experience_7y (its new wording; the IT path reads it) and Asked-for line were written during the grill.
    • src/lib/screen.ts's mixed-levels exception in dependency-rules.md is dated v1.2.2; this slice need not touch it, and the milestone's close will.
    • The 2026-10-18 Done when, kept: no answer in the declared panel is read by no card; every declared row has its pencil; a change of country leaves no answer the new scope does not read, unmarked; the asking rule's test checks per destination and per option.

    Tickets

    • #134 The German shortage Blue Card gains its IT path, and the seven-year question has its own words (data repository)
    • #135 The engine names the routes a question is asked for (data repository)
    • #136 A money row answered "Doesn't apply…" shows its answer and its pencil
    • #137 A change of country names the answers it set aside
    • #138 The asking rule's test checks every destination and every rung (data repository)
    • #139 A question says which routes ask it: the asked-for line

    Comments

    OytunOnal · 21 Oct 2026

    Ruling, 2026-10-21 (the human, during #134's build): a disjunction is judged path by path.

    Built on #134's branch (f5b14af, OytunOnal/permit-rulebook-data): a failed any is a hard fail only when every one of its paths holds a hard-failing criterion. A path that failed only on improvable answers, or only by a bounded gap, keeps the route open.

    Why: with the IT path inside an any that also reads qualification and occupation_it, the old rule judged the disjunction by all its fields together and closed the shortage Blue Card for 5 of s5e's 400 readers whose only shortfall was German recognition ("not yet", "partial"), which they can still improve.

    Accepted as correct, beyond the shortage card: against master, s5e's 400 readers move in 95 rows. Besides the shortage card (18 hard fail → held, 1 → met, 1 held → within reach), 75 rows go from hard fail to held on five routes whose "qualification or experience" disjunction has a path reading only experience_7y: es-blue-card 20, fr-talent-blue-card 19, es-ict 14, es-highly-qualified 12, nl-blue-card 10. A reader without a degree who is short of that rung can reach it by working longer, so by GLOSSARY's Hard fail they are "not yet", not closed. The old verdict was the same bug, already shipped.

    OytunOnal · 21 Oct 2026

    Ruling, 2026-10-21 (the human, during #138's build): questions-do-work's per-destination check draws the passport from every passport, the EU included; the existing all-destinations check keeps its third-country pool. With the narrow pool, citizenship failed on Germany and Spain only because no EU passport was offered. (France's experience_7y ruling is on #89.)

    OytunOnal · 21 Oct 2026

    Ruling, 2026-10-21 (the human, during #139's build): the route page asks for rules inside a disjunction's paths too. Once occupation_shortage moved inside the shortage Blue Card's degree path, its route page stopped drawing "What the checker asks" for it, and the shortage-list door ("Check the shortage groups in section 18g…") was gone from /germany/eu-blue-card-shortage-occupation. The page now draws that box for an unquoted rule inside a disjunction's paths, as it does for one at the top level; a disjunction carrying its own quote covers its paths. Built in 48e8f4b. Its reach: the shortage card's page regains the box with two doors (Anabin, § 18g); the Spanish and Dutch Blue Card pages gain a box with no door ("Did you graduate … within the last 3 years?", "Your highest completed qualification?"); no page lost a box or a door.

    #144 A data test reads the live watch state, so a Dutch outage stops every check and every deploy

    real use #147real-green

    Repository: OytunOnal/permit-rulebook-data.

    Repository: OytunOnal/permit-rulebook-data.

    Found by #125's real use, 2026-10-21; taken now, out of order, on the human's word.

    What a reader sees

    Today's site was not published. While any Dutch source is down, every data check and every deploy is red, so the page a reader reads stops moving forward: no new data, no new code, #133's merge included.

    Why

    tests/s14.test.ts › "s14 — s11's freshness sentence is not disturbed (point 7)" › "over the shipped state, no Dutch source is named" asserts against the shipped watch state (watch/state.json), which the daily watch rewrites. Today wetten-wav-artikel-8 (wetten.overheid.nl, HTTP 403, outage since 10-16) is down, the freshness sentence names "A Dutch source did not answer on the …", and the case fails:

    • the check after the watch: https://github.com/OytunOnal/permit-rulebook-data/actions/runs/39583598752 (opened data#40 check failed on master);
    • the watch's dispatched deploy: https://github.com/OytunOnal/permit-rulebook/actions/runs/39583589728 (opened #140 deploy run failed; "Today's page was not published.").

    A test that today's world can turn red is a check content can break (CODING_STANDARDS.md, Tests).

    Done when

    • The case asserts its decision (s11's freshness sentence is not disturbed when no Dutch source lapses) against a fixed state written for it, never the shipped one; with a fixed state that names a Dutch outage, the sentence names it.
    • No other data test reads the shipped watch/state.json to decide a pass; any that does is named here and fixed the same way.
    • data#40 and #140 close themselves on the next green check and the next green scheduled deploy.

    Comments

    OytunOnal · 21 Oct 2026

    Grill of #144, 2026-10-21. Rulings as the human makes them.

    1. One ticket, no spec. One bug in the data repository with no new screen, so Spine's steps 1–6 are skipped. The human runs /implement 144 on a branch of its own in OytunOnal/permit-rulebook-data; reviews, the fix round and the merge follow from step 7.
    2. Two fixed states replace the shipped one in s14's case. "Over the shipped state, no Dutch source is named" (tests/s14.test.ts, s11's freshness sentence, point 7) stops reading watch/state.json. It reads two states written in the test instead:
    3. only a German source did not answer on the last run, with none of the five IND pages listed: the freshness sentence names a German source and no Dutch one;
    4. wetten-wav-artikel-8, a machine-read Dutch page, did not answer on the last run: the sentence names a Dutch source, proving the first state's "not Dutch" is a decision.
    5. The never-read check moves onto the fixed states. On both, every entry was read at least once, so unreadNeverRead is empty; nothing in the case reads the shipped state.
    6. The two edge cases below stay as they are, outside #144: neither goes red from an outage alone.
    7. A coding standard names it. Step 9's docs commit on site master adds under CODING_STANDARDS.md's Tests: "A test decides its pass on a state it writes itself, never on one a scheduled run rewrites (the shipped watch/state.json). The quote gate is the exception: it is meant to go red when a page changes."
    8. Live today, by hand. A data merge does not rebuild the site, and site master is pinned to data 2310103, which still carries the old s14. Right after the merge the session writes one repository_dispatch command carrying the merged data commit's sha, and the human runs it with ! (deploy dispatches are blocked for the session). A green deploy writes that commit into data.lock, puts #133 (fec4586) live and starts #146.
    9. The second "Done when", read as ruled (review of OytunOnal/permit-rulebook-data#41): no other data test that an outage can turn red reads the shipped watch/state.json; the sweep below proves it. The quote gate keeps reading the shipped state, since its job is to go red when a page's text changes, and so do the structure cases.

    Sweep of every data test that reads the shipped state (the second "Done when"): only s14.test.ts:190 can turn red from an outage or a late run alone. The rest are the quote gate, which is meant to go red when a page's text changes, or structure that an outage leaves alone: a failed fetch keeps the entry's snapshot, retrieved_at and slice_read (src/watch/core.ts:405, early returns at 467/478/498), and no run removes an entry. npm run check only warns about unread sources (src/watch/cli-coverage.ts). Two edge cases are not red today: s11.test.ts:193 (entries predate last_run) goes red only if nearly every page changes on one run day; watch.test.ts:777 stays red after a slice move until a re-read succeeds.

    Wording: the fixed states say "did not answer on the last run", not Lapse or Outage (GLOSSARY): those need the last seven mornings, and a single state's unread list knows only the last run. wetten-wav-artikel-8 has been out since 10-16, an Outage.
