    #151 The reader's words on every screen: what the checker asks, whose page a link opens, headings that read as sentences

    from #92real use #162real-green

    Specced from #92 (with #93, #29, the v1.2.1 gate's F5 and P4, and #146's composed heading folded in). The grill's seventeen rulings are on #92: https://github.com/OytunOnal/permit-rulebook/issues/92#issuecomment-6317737700. Bump Z (bug), no ADR.

    Specced from #92 (with #93, #29, the v1.2.1 gate's F5 and P4, and #146's composed heading folded in). The grill's seventeen rulings are on #92: https://github.com/OytunOnal/permit-rulebook/issues/92#issuecomment-6317737700. Bump Z (bug), no ADR.

    Repositories: both, the site and OytunOnal/permit-rulebook-data.

    Problem Statement

    A reader meets the dataset's words where they expect sentences:

    • Headings and counts speak the model's language: "the Netherlands: 6 routes scored, 1 quoted.", "quoted and dated · scored — 2 conditions this interview did not ask", "Your 9 answers were compared against 12 published rule sets.", and "scored against your answers" on pages where they have answered nothing.
    • A heading that starts with "the Netherlands" starts in lowercase, here and on the interview's arrival line; the route page's description says "Netherlands's".
    • On the all-four path a situation option reads "an employer there itself", because "there" is handed to a phrase built for a country's name.
    • The shortage card's heading runs two paths together: "A university degree, full German recognition and an occupation on Germany's shortage list or work in information technology and 3 to under 5 or 5+ years of related experience in the last seven". The reader cannot tell which "and"s and "or"s group.
    • Shorthand goes unexplained: "§ 18g AufenthG" bare on results cards, "ISCO-08 codes 132, 133, 134, 21…" in help text, MVV, IELTS, Schengen, and language levels with no scale named.
    • One recognition option covers two situations, "Not assessed / not recognised", so a reader with a degree nobody has assessed picks "I don't know" instead.
    • Quotes taken from buzer.de, a consolidated text and not the authority, carry the same "Official page ↗" as bamf.de; nine of seventeen say "(consolidated mirror, buzer.de)" and eight do not.
    • The spouse question asks about "an Opportunity Card" before any screen has said what one is.
    • A reader from Puerto Rico who types it in the passport search gets "No country matches", with no word about the passport they hold.

    Solution

    Every screen outside /data/ speaks the reader's words. Where the reader has answered, a route is "checked against your answers"; where they have not, the page says what the checker asks. A country's name opens a sentence with a capital. A phrase built for a place is never handed "there". A criterion with two paths is named by its own label and lists each path on its own line. Shorthand is explained on first use on each page, through the one gloss the route page already uses. Two recognition situations get two options. A link to a page that is not the authority's says what it is. A question that names a route says what the route is. A territory typed into the passport search is answered with the passport its people hold.

    User Stories

    1. As a reader on a results card, I want "quoted and dated · checked against your answers", so that I know the route was measured against what I told the checker.
    2. As a reader on a results card for a route with unasked conditions, I want "quoted and dated · checked against your answers, except 2 conditions we don't ask about", so that I know what the checker left to me.
    3. As a reader on such a card where some unasked conditions are our reading, I want "— 1 of them in our own words, not the authority's", so that I can tell our words from the authority's.
    4. As a reader on a results card for a quoted-only route, I want "quoted and dated · not checked against your answers", so that I do not mistake a quote for a verdict.
    5. As a reader on a country page, I want the h1 "The Netherlands: 7 routes. The checker asks about 6.", so that I know what I can do with this country's routes before answering anything.
    6. As a reader on a country or route page, I want the scope line to say "the checker asks about all of it", "the checker asks about it, except 2 conditions" or "the checker does not ask about it", so that the page promises nothing about answers I have not given.
    7. As a reader on the country page, I want the section for quoted-only routes headed "Quoted here, not checked", so that the heading uses the same verb as every card.
    8. As a reader of a route that is not open for my country yet, I want "Not checked for X yet", so that the wording matches the rest of the site.
    9. As a reader on the results page, I want "Your 9 answers were checked against the published rules of 12 routes.", so that I count routes, the thing I came for, not "rule sets".
    10. As a reader of a quoted-only route's reason, I want "This route is not checked against your answers", so that the dataset's own text speaks the site's verb.
    11. As a reader of a route's reason, I want "we don't ask" where it said "this interview", so that the site speaks as itself.
    12. As someone who sees a shared link, I want the social card to read "… · 29 routes · the checker asks about 23", so that the card makes the promise the site keeps.
    13. As someone who finds the home page in a search result, I want its description to count routes the same way, so that the first words I read are the reader's words.
    14. As a reader of the Netherlands' country page, I want the h1 to start "The Netherlands", so that it reads as a sentence.
    15. As a reader arriving with the Netherlands already on the record, I want "The Netherlands is on the record.", so that the arrival line reads as a sentence.
    16. As a reader whose search result shows a route page's description, I want "the Netherlands' Highly skilled migrant …", so that the name keeps its article and its possessive is right.
    17. As a reader of a verdict line that names the Netherlands, I want the article and capital right wherever the name falls in the sentence, so that no line reads "This route is Netherlands".
    18. As a reader on the all-four path, I want the contract option to read "An employment contract directly with an employer in the country you move to — including one you already hold — not a transfer to a branch on a contract you hold abroad.", so that it reads as a sentence before I have chosen a country.
    19. As a reader who has chosen Germany, I want the same option to read "… directly with an employer in Germany …", so that it names my country.
    20. As a reader on the all-four path, I want the transfer option to read "… moves you to its branch in the country you move to.", so that no option says "there" about a place it has not named.
    21. As a reader on the shortage route's page, I want the condition headed "Recognised degree or IT experience", so that I see at once it is one condition with a choice.
    22. As a reader of that card, I want "Either path meets it:" followed by "Recognised degree: …" and "IT specialist: …" on lines of their own, so that I can see which requirements belong together.
    23. As a reader of the IT path, I want "at least 3 years of related experience in the last seven", so that I am not asked to read a list of rungs.
    24. As a reader of a results card whose verdict line names a criterion with paths, I want it named by the same label, so that the results and the route page agree.
    25. As a reader of a results card, I want "§ 18g AufenthG (the Residence Act)" on first use on the page, so that the citation is explained where I read it, as it already is on the route page.
    26. As a reader of a Dutch route, I want "MVV (the Dutch entry visa)" on first use, so that I know what the statement asks of me.
    27. As a reader of the Dutch language statement, I want "IELTS (an English-language test)" on first use, so that I know what it names.
    28. As a reader of the stay-history question, I want "Schengen (the EU's border-free travel area)" on first use, so that the question does not assume I know it.
    29. As a reader of the shortage question's help, I want "the occupation groups on Germany's shortage list (ISCO-08 codes 132, 133, 134, 21…)", so that I know what the codes are for before I look them up.
    30. As a reader of a language question, I want it to name its scale once, "levels on the European language scale (CEFR)", so that A1 to C1 mean something.
    31. As a reader choosing "German below A1" or "English below B2", I want a gloss like the other six options have, so that every option reads the same way.
    32. As a reader whose degree has never been assessed, I want an option "Not assessed yet", so that I am not pushed to "I don't know".
    33. As a reader whose degree was assessed and refused, I want an option "Assessed and not recognised", so that my situation is named.
    34. As a returning reader whose saved record holds the old combined answer, I want the recognition question asked again, so that my answer is one of the options on screen.
    35. As a reader of a quote from buzer.de, I want its link to read "Consolidated text on buzer.de ↗", so that I know it is not the authority's own page.
    36. As a reader of the shortage question, I want its learn link to say the same, so that the disclosure is everywhere the link is.
    37. As a reader of a quote from an authority's own site, I want "Official page ↗", so that the label means what it says.
    38. As a contributor adding a source on a new host, I want the check to fail until the host is on the authority list or the consolidated-text list, so that a mirror cannot ship under "Official page".
    39. As a reader of the spouse question, I want "Is your spouse or partner also applying for Germany's Opportunity Card — the permit to come and look for work — and eligible for one?", so that I know what I am asked about.
    40. As a reader of the spouse question, I want a learn link to the card's official page, so that I can read more before I answer.
    41. As a contributor adding a question that names a route, I want to name it through a slot that brings the route's phrase with it, and the check to fail on a route name typed by hand, so that no question names a route the reader has not met.
    42. As a reader from Puerto Rico, I want the search to answer "Puerto Rico is not on this list: people from Puerto Rico hold a passport of the United States. Choose United States.", so that I know which row is mine.
    43. As a reader typing a name that is neither a country nor a territory, I want today's "No country matches. Try fewer letters, or the country's own name.", so that the territory line appears only when it is true.
    44. As a reader typing "Puerto Rico", I want no row to match, so that the search never shows a country I did not type.
    45. As a contributor, I want every criterion with paths and every path to carry a label, so that no card falls back to joining the paths in one line.
    46. As a contributor, I want a criterion that accepts two or more rungs of one ordered answer to need a short_reason, so that no card prints a list of rungs.
    47. As a maintainer, I want every place that prints a country's name in running text to use one data-package function, so that the article, the capital and the possessive are decided once.
    48. As a maintainer, I want "scored", "conditions this interview did not ask" and "rule sets" kept in code and on /data/ only, so that the model's words stay where the model is described.
    49. As a reader of /data/, I want its counts and gate lines unchanged, so that the page that describes the dataset still uses the dataset's terms.

    Implementation Decisions

    Words (rulings 2–5, 17).

    • The reader's verb is "checked"; "scored", "rule set(s)" and "this interview" leave every reader screen but /data/. "Condition" stays the reader's word.
    • The scope line has two forms, chosen by whether the reader has answered. Results card: "quoted and dated · checked against your answers" · "quoted and dated · checked against your answers, except N conditions we don't ask about" · "quoted and dated · not checked against your answers". Country and route pages: "quoted and dated · the checker asks about all of it" · "… the checker asks about it, except N conditions" · "… the checker does not ask about it". Where unasked limbs are Route readings, both forms end "— M of them in our own words, not the authority's"; where every unasked limb is a Route reading, the tail is ", in our own words, not the authority's" (the human's ruling on #153's choices, 2026-10-21).
    • The data package's scope-line function takes which form it is asked for; the site picks the form by page. The site's own sentences that carried "scored" ("Quoted here, not scored", "Not scored for X yet", "No scored route in …", "is quoted here but not scored", "the checker does not score", "we state and do not score", "the ones quoted and not scored") take "checked" or "asks" by the same rule; their words stay in the site's copy module.
    • Country h1: "<Country>: N routes. The checker asks about M." The social card and the home page's description count the same way ("29 routes · the checker asks about 23").
    • Results masthead: "Your N answers were checked against the published rules of M routes."
    • Dataset text in the data repository: the six quoted-only reasons' "This route is not scored" becomes "This route is not checked against your answers"; the two "this interview" phrases become "we don't ask"; summaries that say "Quoted here, not scored" follow.
    • /data/ keeps its terms: its lede, its Routes row, its gate lines, its meta description and its JSON-LD description.
    • GLOSSARY's Scope statement already carries both forms and the avoid-list (committed with The Road before the build).

    A country's name in a sentence (ruling 6).

    • One data-package function returns a country's phrase with its article from the country list, in three shapes: within a sentence ("the Netherlands"), at a sentence or heading start ("The Netherlands"), and possessive ("the Netherlands'"). It throws on a country it does not know.
    • Every reader place that prints a country's name in running text uses it: the country h1, the interview's arrival line, the route page's description, the verdict lines, the unscored-route lines. The site's own article set and possessive helper are deleted.
    • The country list gives an article to every name that takes one in English running text (today only the Netherlands carries one; the United States, the United Kingdom, the Philippines, the Gambia, the Bahamas, the Maldives, the United Arab Emirates, the Dominican Republic, the Central African Republic and the other "Republic"/"Islands"/plural names need it). The phrase is only as right as that list.
    • Labels that are a country's name alone (a results section heading, a chip, a list row, /data/'s row label) keep the bare name.

    Place slots (ruling 7).

    • The two situation phrases with a place slot are rewritten to read with any place: "An employment contract directly with an employer {place} — including one you already hold — not a transfer to a branch on a contract you hold abroad." and "Your contract stays with the company abroad and it moves you to its branch {place}."
    • The filler gives "in <the country's phrase>" when a place is known and "in the country you move to" when none is; "there" is never a fill.

    Criteria with paths (rulings 12–13).

    • A criterion with paths is named, in every one-line form (the route card's heading, the results' verdict line), by its own label, capitalised at a heading start: "Recognised degree or IT experience". Where the engine has decided the reader's nearest path, the results' verdict line names what that path still needs instead ("Needs full German recognition and at least €45,934.20/year"), which tells the reader more (the human's ruling at the fix round, 2026-10-21).
    • The route card's body for it is "Either path meets it:" and one line per path, "<path label>: <the path's criteria joined with 'and'>". The words are the site's copy; the labels are the dataset's.
    • The IT path's experience criterion gets the short_reason "at least 3 years of related experience in the last seven".
    • An enum question may be marked ordered in the dataset: its options run from least to most of one amount. Today that is the two experience questions; a contributor marks any other ladder the same way.

    Shorthand (ruling 8).

    • Route names keep their form: the plain name, then the citation in brackets; "§" stays (s32 amendment 6).
    • The one gloss module runs over all our text a reader sees: the route page (as today), the results card's citations, question labels, help and learn labels, and statements. Never over a quote.
    • Its list grows: MVV (the Dutch entry visa), IELTS (an English-language test), Schengen (the EU's border-free travel area), ISCO-08 (the international occupation codes). Each page keeps its own memory of what it has explained.
    • Each language question names its scale once in its own text: "levels on the European language scale (CEFR)". "German below A1" and "English below B2" get a short gloss like the other six options.
    • The shortage question's learn label says what the codes are for: "the occupation groups on Germany's shortage list (ISCO-08 codes 132, 133, 134, 21…)".

    Recognition (ruling 9).

    • recognition_de's not_yet is replaced by two values, "Not assessed yet" (short "a qualification not yet assessed in Germany") and "Assessed and not recognised" (short "a qualification Germany assessed and did not recognise"). Every criterion and points row treats both as not_yet was treated; no next-step text is added.
    • A saved record holding not_yet loses that answer and the question is asked again (the record's restore keeps only offered values). Fixtures move to the new values.

    Consolidated text (ruling 10).

    • The data package holds two host lists, authority hosts and consolidated-text hosts (today buzer.de alone), and one function telling which kind a source URL's host is. The words are the site's (a screen word never lives in the data): "Official page" for an authority host, "Consolidated text on <host>" for a consolidated-text host, in the site's copy module.
    • Every link the site prints to a source takes its label that way: route-page sources, results-card sources, notice links where no learn label is given, and question learn links whose label names no host. The site's constant "Official page" goes.
    • The nine "(consolidated mirror, buzer.de)" suffixes leave legal_basis.
    • buzer.de stays as the source: the official text refuses the watch and times out from Türkiye, and a person re-checks the mirrored quotes quarterly. GLOSSARY's Consolidated text names it.

    Questions that name a route (ruling 11).

    • partner_ck's label becomes "Is your spouse or partner also applying for Germany's Opportunity Card — the permit to come and look for work — and eligible for one?", and the question gains a learn link to the card's official page; its subject and option shorts follow.
    • Each route a question can name carries a short phrase saying what it is (the Opportunity Card's: "the permit to come and look for work"). A question names a route only through a slot holding the route's id, which the data package fills, as it fills a place, with the route's country, name and phrase: "Germany's Opportunity Card — the permit to come and look for work". So the phrase is there by construction, and the one check forbids rather than demands: a route's plain name typed literally into a question's text fails (CODING_STANDARDS, "none that demands content"; the human's ruling at the standards read, 2026-10-21).

    Territories (rulings 14–15).

    • No territory becomes a search alias or a row.
    • Each territory the country list excludes carries the passport its people hold. The search's no-match line, when what the reader typed is a territory's name, reads "<Territory> is not on this list: people from <Territory> hold a passport of <the country's phrase>. Choose <Country>." ("… hold a passport of the United States. Choose United States."). The grill's example said "a United States passport"; built from a name, that form breaks for most countries ("a Denmark passport"), so the line takes the country's phrase instead (the human's ruling at the

    Tickets

    • #152 A country's name opens a sentence with a capital
    • #153 "Checked", not "scored": the reader's words for what the checker asks
    • #154 No place is filled with "there"
    • #155 A criterion with paths is named by its label, one path per line
    • #156 Shorthand explained on first use, on every page
    • #157 Two recognition options: not assessed yet, and assessed and not recognised
    • #158 A link to a consolidated text says so
    • #159 A question names a route through its slot, which says what the route is
    • #160 A territory typed in the passport search names its passport

    #167 The verdict in one module, saying only what it knows

    from #55real use #180real-green

    Specced from #55 (with #90, #95, the v1.2.1 gate's B1 and adjustments 1 and 2, #126's masthead, and the page's line limit folded in). The grill's seventeen rulings are on #55: https://github.com/OytunOnal/permit-rulebook/issues/55#issuecomment-6339075134. Bump Z (bug), no ADR.

    Specced from #55 (with #90, #95, the v1.2.1 gate's B1 and adjustments 1 and 2, #126's masthead, and the page's line limit folded in). The grill's seventeen rulings are on #55: https://github.com/OytunOnal/permit-rulebook/issues/55#issuecomment-6339075134. Bump Z (bug), no ADR.

    Repositories: both, the site and OytunOnal/permit-rulebook-data.

    Problem Statement

    A reader who finishes the interview reads a verdict that says more than the product knows, and points at things that are not there:

    • On the lowest salary band ("under €33,085.09") the headline reads "0 routes look open. One is short by €45,630/year." — the route's whole threshold given as the reader's shortfall. On the band above it prints the band's widest gap as a fact ("short by €4,274/year") while the rail beside it says "up to €4,273.64 short". One gap, two numbers.
    • The rail goes blank exactly when the reader is furthest from the line: "under €33,085.09" draws nothing, and "€59,373 or more" is a blue bar on one card and an empty track with a lone tick on the next, for the same answer.
    • "Steps that would unlock more" lists an intra-corporate transfer and a research hosting agreement beside full German recognition. A reader cannot take a transfer; their employer decides it. The hostile walk's headline names "a research hosting agreement" as the nearest step to a reader who declared a job offer in Spain.
    • An explorer with no offer reads "Nothing open yet — 8 steps would change that", none of which they can use, while 14 of their 23 not-yet rows say "Needs a job offer". The step is on the screen as a reason and missing as a step.
    • The step headings are set in the link blue and are not links; the three bordered counters read as tabs and do nothing when clicked.
    • The "criteria met" badge sits top right on a desktop and under a two-line title on a phone.
    • On a one-country path, Back from the verdict skips the last question: the verdict never writes a history entry.

    Behind them, the developer's problem: about 680 lines inside the page's bundled script decide which heading a route sits under, in what order, which sections open, and assemble each card from nineteen shallow *Html pieces. The two layouts compose their sections separately, a rule-shaped decision (isStepGated, "engine parity") sits in the site, a dozen reader-facing words are typed into the script, and the masthead's words share a file with the interview's flow. The page is 2,224 lines against a 1,000-line limit. Every fix above would touch the page, the card pieces and the masthead at once.

    Solution

    One module returns the whole verdict from the dataset, the answers and the arrival; the page paints it. Inside it, the verdict says only what it knows:

    • A bounded gap is "up to", in one exact form, everywhere it is printed. A band open at the bottom names the line ("needs €45,630/year or more"), never the threshold as a shortfall.
    • Every rail draws the reader's answer: a bar for a bounded band, a point at the rail's edge for an open one, with the card's own words beside it.
    • Steps are what the reader can do. A transfer or a hosting agreement is an arranged answer, said apart from the steps in one line.
    • An explorer with no offer reads, per country, what a job offer would do: how many more routes would be checked, and on what — never "would be met".
    • Step headings are ink; the counters are a plain summary line; the badge has one home at every width.
    • Every verdict writes its history entry, so Back lands where the reader was.

    User Stories

    1. As a reader on the lowest salary band, I want the headline to name the route's line ("One needs €45,630/year or more."), so that I am not told I am short by my whole salary.
    2. As a reader on a bounded band, I want the headline to say "One is short by up to €4,273.64/year.", so that it says what the card says and no more.
    3. As a reader comparing the headline with the card and the rail, I want one gap printed one way in all three, so that I can trust that they mean the same number.
    4. As a reader, I want a gap printed exactly, cents only when it has them, with its period, so that I can check it against the two quoted amounts it is the difference of.
    5. As a reader whose gap is €4,273.40, I want "up to €4,273.40", never "up to €4,273", so that "up to" never understates.
    6. As a reader on a band open at the bottom, I want the card to say "Asks €45,630/year or more on salary — your answer is under €33,085.09.", so that it names the line and my answer.
    7. As a reader on a band open at the bottom, I want the rail to put a point at its left edge captioned "below the amount", so that my answer is drawn where it lies.
    8. As a reader on a band open at the top ("€59,373 or more"), I want every rail to draw a point at its right edge captioned "above the amount", so that the same answer looks the same on every card.
    9. As a reader on a bounded band, I want every rail to draw my band as a bar, so that I see where it sits against the line.
    10. As a reader, I never want a tick on an empty track to stand for my answer, so that the rail never looks blank when I am furthest from the line.
    11. As an offer-holder, I want the steps block to list only what I can do (recognition, language, experience, funds), so that I am not offered my employer's decisions as steps.
    12. As a reader, I want "Nearest:" in the headline to name only a step I can take, so that the nearest step is never a transfer I cannot request.
    13. As a reader whose employer has a branch abroad, I want one line under the steps saying what a different situation would open ("With a different situation: an intra-corporate transfer would open 1 route in Germany …"), so that I still learn it.
    14. As a reader for whom no step opens anything but an arranged answer would, I want the headline to read "Nothing open on these answers." with its way forward, so that it does not count what I cannot do as a step.
    15. As a reader on the several-country layout, I want the arranged-answer line in each country's section naming only that country's routes, so that it sits where the steps do.
    16. As a reader, I want a country's tally to count only steps, so that "2 unlocking steps" means two things I can do.
    17. As an explorer with no offer, I want the steps block to open with "With a job offer in Germany: 4 more routes would be checked — on the salary it pays, and your recognition.", so that the one step that matters most to me is on the screen as a step.
    18. As an explorer, I want that line per country, naming what those routes would ask next, so that I know what an offer would lead to.
    19. As an explorer, I never want that line to say "would be met" or "within reach", so that nothing resting on questions I have not answered is promised.
    20. As an explorer, I want the headline's step count and "Nearest:" to leave the offer line out, so that the headline still counts only what provably changes a verdict.
    21. As an offer-holder, I never want to see the offer line, so that I am not told to get what I have.
    22. As a reader, I want step headings set in ink, so that I do not click them expecting a link.
    23. As a reader, I want the counters ("1 open · 0 within reach · 7 not yet") as a plain line with no border, so that they do not read as tabs.
    24. As a reader on a phone or a desktop, I want the status badge on the card's first line, above the title, beside the country's name, so that it is in one place at every width and read first.
    25. As a reader on the one-country layout, I want the badge alone on that first line, so that the card still opens with its status.
    26. As a reader on a phone, I want the first paint to hold still when the badge moves, so that nothing jumps under my thumb.
    27. As a reader on a one-country path, I want Back from the verdict to land on the last question I answered, so that I can change it.
    28. As a reader on any path, I want every verdict to be one entry in the browser's history, so that Back and Forward behave the same on both layouts.
    29. As a reader who came from a route page, I want that route marked and first in its group, as today, so that the move changes nothing I rely on.
    30. As a reader, I want every verdict word I read to be the product's copy, so that a word is changed in one place and said the same everywhere.
    31. As a reader of a not-yet group, I want "N routes need a job offer — see the steps above" to point up only when something stands above, so that it never points at nothing.
    32. As a screen-reader user, I want the verdict announced as today, from the same masthead it draws, so that what I hear is what is written.
    33. As a keyboard user, I want the headline to take the focus after a verdict as today, so that I do not tab through the header to reach it.
    34. As the developer, I want one call that returns the whole verdict as data, so that grouping, order and open state are tested in Node.
    35. As the developer, I want one call that turns the verdict into the body the page paints, so that the markup is tested without a browser.
    36. As the developer, I want the card pieces internal to the verdict module, so that a card block changes in one place and tests reach it only through the entry.
    37. As the developer, I want the verdict screen's masthead inside the verdict module, so that the headline and "Nearest:" are composed beside the sections they summarise.
    38. As the developer, I want the question screens' masthead beside the question card, so that the verdict module carries no interview words.
    39. As the developer, I want the interview's flow and history file to hold no words, so that it is one level and its exception goes.
    40. As the developer, I want whether a path answer is a step to be a property of the dataset, so that no list of fields lives in the site.
    41. As the developer, I want the offer line's count computed by the engine beside unlocks, so that both counterfactuals live in the domain and are tested there.
    42. As the developer, I want the step-gated decision computed by the engine, so that no rule is decided in the site.
    43. As the developer, I want the verdict's styles beside the module, from tokens.css, so that the card's look and its markup change together.
    44. As the developer, I want the page smaller by the verdict's code and styles, and what is still over 1,000 lines written onto #56, so that the limit has an owner.
    45. As the developer, I want the move proven to change nothing before any fix lands, so that a review can tell a deliberate change from a slip.
    46. As the maintainer of the dataset, I want a transfer and a hosting agreement marked as arranged answers in the dataset, so that the screen's split follows the data.
    47. As the maintainer of the dataset, I want the new mark to be an optional field with a schema bump, so that the contract says it.

    Implementation Decisions

    The verdict module (rulings 1, 5, 6).

    • A deep module of its own folder, declared under Modules in dependency-rules.md, level presentation. Its entry exports verdictOf, verdictHtml and their types, nothing else.
    • verdictOf(dataset, answers, interview) returns the verdict as data. interview carries the id of the route the reader came from (nothing else of the arrival) and the interview's answered count. The verdict holds:
    • which screen it is: answered outright by a notice, or compared — as the flow's action (answered-outright or compare);
    • that screen's whole masthead: the headline markup, the subline, the subline with its door where it has one, and the sentence the live region announces;
    • the layout: one country flat, or country sections. A section has its open state, its tally and its groups (open, unsettled, within reach, steps, not yet, not open to your passport); a group lists its routes in order, each with its card's markup.
    • verdictHtml(verdict) composes the body the page paints.
    • Inside: the card pieces and the step arrangement, whole; the verdict screens' masthead (the result and notice cases, EXPLORE_SENTENCE, the headline's markup, the answers-checked line); the verdict-only parts of the situations module (unscoredTally, unscoredVerdict, leadCountry, leads, namedFirst); and the composition now in renderResults, the two layouts sharing one section policy.
    • Outside, imported inward: the quote, reason and gloss modules (shared with the other pages) and what is left of the situations module (the question card's).
    • The page keeps what has a side effect: painting, the arrival line (shared by every screen, #56's), the announcement, the landing and the focus, recording the screen, the listeners. renderResults becomes a call to verdictOf, the flow's advance, a paint, and the page's side effects.
    • Every verdict word moves to the site's copy module. No reader-facing word of the verdict is typed in the page or the module.
    • The card's boolean arguments (with or without the country, unsettled or not) become an option named for what it sets.

    The masthead split (rulings 6, #126).

    • The results and notice screens carry their masthead as data, from verdictOf. Only compare and answered-outright can produce one, so no question screen can carry a claim about a comparison — B4's guarantee, by type.
    • The question screens' masthead (the intro headline and both sublines) goes beside the question card; its words to the copy module. The page draws one or the other by the screen's kind.
    • claimsAComparison moves into the flow's test, its only user.
    • The flow and history file keeps the flow and the history, takes UnscoredVerdict, and is tagged application. Its exception in dependency-rules.md (v1.2.2) is deleted.

    Every verdict records its screen (ruling 4). The page's one paint path records the verdict's screen for both layouts. Today the one-country branch returns before it does.

    The gap (rulings 2, 3, 11).

    • One function formats a gap: the dataset's formatEURPer on the unrounded gap — exact, cents only when present, always with its period. The headline, the card's "Short by up to …", the rail's caption and a step's "would come within reach (…)" all print it. No rounding anywhere a gap is printed.
    • Whether a band is open at the bottom is read from the band (no lower edge), never from a list of fields.
    • Headline emphasis: a bounded band, "One is short by up to <gap>."; a band open at the bottom, "One needs <threshold> or more."; points, unchanged ("One is N points away.").
    • Card, on a band open at the bottom: "Asks <threshold> or more on <field> — your answer is <band label>." in place of "Short by up to …". Rail row: "below the amount".

    The rail (adjustment 1, ruling 11). Every rail draws the reader's band: a bounded band as its bar; an open band as a point at the rail's edge (left for open at the bottom, right for open at the top) with the row's caption ("below the amount" / "above the amount"). A tick on an empty track never stands for an answer. The cause today: a band open at one end is given an invented far edge (80% of the lowest amount, 130% of the highest), and where the band's real edge lies past it the bar has negative width.

    Steps and arranged answers (rulings 7, 14, 16; #90).

    • Data: the situation field's transfer and hosting-agreement options carry is_arranged: true, an optional option field named like is_fallback, is_unknown, is_qualifier. Schema 0.8.3. A job offer and the fallback carry none.
    • The steps block and "Nearest:" take only steps: improvable answers and path answers that are not arranged. The headline's "N steps would change that" counts steps only.
    • What an arranged answer would open is said in one line after the steps, in its own paragraph style, not a step card: "With a different situation: <arranged answer> would open <n> route(s) in <country> …". Alone in that place, under no heading, when there are no steps; per country on the several-country layout, naming only that country's routes.
    • Where no step opens anything but an arranged answer would, the headline is "Nothing open on these answers." with its explore sentence.
    • A country's tally counts steps only.
    • The not-yet group's "— see the steps above" appears only when the steps block, the offer line or the arranged line stands above it.

    The offer line (rulings 8, 17; adjustment 2).

    • On the explorer path only (the situation answer is the fallback), the steps block opens with one line per country: "With a job offer in <country>: <n> more route(s) would be checked — on <what they would ask next>."
    • The engine computes it, beside unlocks: a pure function of the dataset and the answers returning, per country, the routes a job offer frees from their only hard fail and the fields they would then ask (the questions left unanswered). Its name says what it returns (routesAnOfferFrees). "A job offer" is read from the dataset — the situation answer that is neither arranged nor the fallback — never typed.
    • It never says "met" or "within reach", is not counted in the headline's step count, and "Nearest:" never names it. The verdict module words it from the copy module, naming the fields by their short labels.
    • Adjustment 2's "N routes would be met" is not kept: computed as the other steps are, N is 0 in every country on today's data (the explorer persona: DE 4, FR 3, ES 2, NL 2 freed, none met).

    No rule decided in the site. The step-gated decision (a not-yet route whose only genuine failures are path answers, with no live unknown and no moot criterion) moves to the engine beside isPlacedElsewhere, as isStepGated. The verdict module groups by it; it no longer derives it.

    What looks like a control (ruling 9, F4). Step headings are set in ink, the step's name in bold ink. The counters lose their bordered pills and become a plain summary line in the strip's text style. Both from tokens.css.

    The badge (ruling 10, #95). On the card's first line, above the title, beside the country's name, at every width; alone on that line in the one-country layout. Nothing wraps differently by width or title length.

    Styles (ruling 12). The verdict's styles (groups, strip, country sections, not-yet rows, steps, card blocks, card frame, rail, gap and points lines, card foot, scope line) leave the page's style block for a stylesheet inside the module, imported by its entry (the page reaches it only through the entry), every colour from tokens.css, a size from it where its scale has one.

    The page's line limit (ruling 12). This slice takes out the verdict code and its styles and stops there (estimated ~1,300 of 2,224 lines). The build measures the page after the move and writes onto #56 a Done-when line, "index.astro is at most 1,000 lines", with the measured count and what is left.

    Order (ruling 13). The move lands first and changes nothing a reader sees. Each fix lands after it, on the moved module, red first. The data repository's change (is_arranged, routesAnOfferFrees, isStepGated) ships before the site's tickets that read it, through data.lock.

    Testing Decisions

    • A test asserts a decision, not content: which group a route sits in and in what order, which section opens, whether a step is offered, whether a gap is said as "up to" or as a line, whether a rail draws a bar or a point at which edge, whether the headline names a step. Where a test needs words, they are the copy module's, read by name. It decides its pass on the shipped dataset or one it writes, never on the shipped watch state.
    • Seam 1, the verdict module's entry, in Node (site). verdictOf and verdictHtml carry every decision above: grouping, order and open state on both layouts; the masthead (B1's three forms, "Nearest:", the arranged-only headline); the gap's one form; the rail's bar or point and caption; steps against arranged answers; the offer line's presence, absence and wording; the badge's place in the card's markup; the not-yet group's pointer. Every test that imports a piec

    Tickets

    • #168 The step-gated decision is the engine's
    • #169 Arranged answers, and what a job offer frees
    • #170 One call returns the verdict
    • #171 The card pieces and steps go inside the verdict, and their tests cross its entry
    • #172 The masthead splits by screen
    • #173 The verdict's grouping cases run in Node, and the page's remainder goes to #56
    • #174 A gap is said one way and drawn on every rail
    • #175 Steps are what the reader can do
    • #176 Steps look like steps, and the badge has one home
    • #177 Every verdict is one history entry

    #6 [new-need] The results screen has no door: ask for feedback at the moment the judgement happens

    closed

    Reported by a reader through the maker (2026-09-10): the product should ask for feedback at some point — as a prompt that opens, or as an icon that is always there.

    Reported by a reader through the maker (2026-09-10): the product should ask for feedback at some point — as a prompt that opens, or as an icon that is always there.

    Where it stands today. The footer of every page carries "Report a wrong value" and "Suggest a route or a change", and a route page repeats the first one in its body, beside the data. The results screen — where a reader actually decides the product is wrong about them — carries neither in its body. So the moment the judgement happens is the moment with no door.

    What was tried and rejected. Two shapes were drawn in docs/spine/design/s7-feedback.html and the maker rejected both (2026-09-10):

    • A, a slip docked to the bottom edge — appears once after the results have been read, never dims the page, closable.
    • B, a centred modal over a dimmed page — the same words, but it stops the reading.

    Neither is the answer. Whatever replaces them has to keep the rules both were written under, because they are what make this product what it is:

    • it never appears before the reader has an answer, and never during the interview;
    • once, ever, remembered on the device;
    • it asks only for the two things the product can act on — a value that is wrong, a thing that should be different — with no rating, no e-mail field, and nothing leaving the device;
    • it says out loud that filing needs a GitHub account (see the sibling issue), because a link that ends at a login screen is a trap rather than a door;
    • it costs no tap target on a 390 px screen that the results already need.

    Not decided: whether the answer is a prompt at all. A quieter option is a line in the results screen's own body, in the page's voice, where the judgement happens — no furniture, no timing, no dismissal state.

    Comments

    OytunOnal · 10 Sep 2026

    Sibling: #7 — the door this prompt would lead to needs a GitHub account. Worth deciding together.

    OytunOnal · 10 Sep 2026

    Built as the quiet option, on the branch feedback-line (20681e6) — not on master, because the 48-hour window is open until 2026-09-11 09:00 and the live site is touched only for a blocker. This is an improvement, so it waits.

    What it is: one line at the end of the results screen, under a hairline rule.

    Wrong about you? A value that does not match its source, or something this screen should do — report a wrong value or suggest a change. Both open GitHub, where filing needs an account.

    It keeps every rule this issue set: it appears only where the reader has an answer to disagree with (a test asserts it is never rendered beside a question), it asks only for the two things the product can act on, nothing leaves the device, nothing is remembered, and the GitHub requirement is said before the click rather than discovered after it — #7 stays open, this only stops it surprising anyone.

    What it gives up, said plainly: it does not arrive, so a reader who does not reach the end of the results never sees it. That was the trade the two drawn shapes were rejected over, and it is the one the product's register can carry.

    Six tests; 300 in the suite.

    #7 [design-flaw] Every way back in needs a GitHub account

    closed

    Every way back into this product ends at github.com, and filing there needs an account.

    Every way back into this product ends at github.com, and filing there needs an account.

    The tracker link on every page, the "Suggest a route or a change" link, and the route pages' "Report a wrong value" all open github.com/OytunOnal/permit-rulebook-data/issues/new/choose. A reader who is not a developer — which is most of the people this product is for, someone weighing a job offer with a non-EU passport — meets a sign-up wall at exactly the moment they were willing to help.

    Why it is still like this. The product has no backend by design: answers never leave the device, and a test asserts it. A form that posts a report needs somewhere to post it, and that is a server. So the cheapest door was the one that already existed.

    What the options cost, as far as they are understood today:

    • A `mailto:` link — no server, no account, works everywhere. Costs a public address that will be scraped, and a report that lands in a mailbox instead of a tracker with labels and history.
    • A hosted form (a third-party service) — no account for the reader, but a third party now receives what a reader types, which is a promise this product has not made and would have to make carefully; it also adds a connect-src/form-action exception to a policy that currently allows neither.
    • A tiny endpoint of our own — full control, and the first server this project has ever run: a deployment, a secret, an abuse surface, and a sentence in the privacy statement that today reads "there is no backend".
    • Leave it, and say so plainly — keep GitHub, but name the requirement at every door rather than letting a reader discover it after the click.

    Blocks nothing, but it caps everything: the feedback prompt in the sibling issue is worth little if the door behind it is locked.

    Comments

    OytunOnal · 10 Sep 2026

    Sibling: #6 — the prompt that would send more people through this door.

    #9 [design-flaw] "Newest value read" is read as "last checked" — the label does not say which of two dates it is

    closed

    Found by the product's own author on 2026-09-15, walking /data/: "footer da eski value'lar… Newest value read 2026-09-10 — bunlar dinamik değil mi?"

    Found by the product's own author on 2026-09-15, walking /data/: "footer da eski value'lar… Newest value read 2026-09-10 — bunlar dinamik değil mi?"

    They are dynamic, and they are right. The dataset holds 183 read dates; the newest is 2026-09-10 and dataset.json last changed that day (29fde08). Nothing in the dataset has changed since. The daily watch confirms every source unchanged and, by design, does not move a value's date when it does: the date beside a value is the day that reading was taken, and moving it on every confirmation would erase "unchanged since X". The day it was last confirmed is the line beside it — "re-read daily — last run 2026-09-15".

    The defect is the label. Two facts sit side by side — last changed 2026-09-10, last checked 2026-09-15 — and "Newest value read" says which one it is only to someone who already knows this product's meaning of "read". The author read it as "last checked". A label the product's own author misreads has not earned its meaning (the same class as s7's "ICT — seconded employee").

    Proposed fix, one page, two rows (src/lib/data-page.ts, the "What it holds today" facts list):

    • Newest value read 2026-09-10 → `Newest value changed 2026-09-10` — retrieved_at moves only when a person updates a value after a flag, which is a change, so the word is accurate.
    • a new row beneath it: `Last checked 2026-09-15`, from the watch's last_run — the fact the reader was looking for, with its own label.

    Left alone on purpose: the "read <date>" beside every quote, the RULES READ stamp, and the footer's "values read between … and …". There "read" means what it says — that sentence was taken from that page on that day — and the footer already prints the last run next to the span.

    Queued after s11 (which is merging today) and s10 (CLS, v1.1's last slice).

    Comments

    OytunOnal · 16 Sep 2026

    Live since s12 (f5df33c, 2026-09-16): 'Newest value changed' and 'Last checked' on /data/.

    #10 [design-flaw] /data/ copy polish: "declared … as ours", "the tracker", two identical dates

    closed

    From the light-mode product critique of s12 on 2026-09-16 (docs/spine/critique-s12-light.md), walking /data/'s "What it holds today" list and the freshness paragraph as the one-pager's persona. No blockers, no friction; three polish items, two older than s12 and in its walked scope:

    From the light-mode product critique of s12 on 2026-09-16 (docs/spine/critique-s12-light.md), walking /data/'s "What it holds today" list and the freshness paragraph as the one-pager's persona. No blockers, no friction; three polish items, two older than s12 and in its walked scope:

    1. "Sentences of ours — 46, declared and shown as ours." "Declared" is the prose-provenance gate's own vocabulary wearing a label; a stranger understands it only after reading the Prose provenance line further down. A plainer value ("46 written by us, and marked as ours" or the like) would let the label stand on its own.
    2. "A source that has moved files an issue in the tracker and a person reads it" — the tracker is named to a reader who has not been introduced to it on this page. The footer's "Report a wrong value" is the door they would want; a link on "the tracker", or the same words the footer uses, would close the gap.
    3. Two identical dates side by side — Dataset version 2026-09-10 beside Newest value changed 2026-09-10. Both true, different facts; today they coincide and the pair invites "why twice". Not actionable until they diverge; recorded so the question is expected rather than a surprise.

    Take with the next copy pass on /data/. Not part of s12's merge.

  10. closed on
    16 Sep 2026

    #11 [design-flaw] Dataset JSON-LD: spatialCoverage as {"@type":"Country"} is "invalid object type" to Google

    closed

    Source: Google Search Console → Datasets, export of 2026-09-16 (Non-critical issues.csv): Invalid object type for field "spatialCoverage", 1 item, validation not started. The dataset itself is valid (1 valid, 0 invalid, every day since 2026-09-11 — the first day Google saw /data/).

    Source: Google Search Console → Datasets, export of 2026-09-16 (Non-critical issues.csv): Invalid object type for field "spatialCoverage", 1 item, validation not started. The dataset itself is valid (1 valid, 0 invalid, every day since 2026-09-11 — the first day Google saw /data/).

    What we send (src/lib/dataset-ld.ts, live on /data/):

    "spatialCoverage": [{"@type":"Country","name":"Germany"}, …]

    Why it is flagged. In schema.org Country is a subtype of Place, so the markup is correct vocabulary — but Google's Dataset parser accepts spatialCoverage only as Text or `{"@type":"Place"}` (its three documented examples: a Place with GeoCoordinates, a Place with GeoShape, or a plain string such as "Tahoe City, CA"). Country is not on its list, hence "invalid object type". Non-critical: the dataset is still indexed and eligible; the property is simply dropped.

    Fix (one line, no copy change): emit the countries as text — spatialCoverage: countries (an array of the four names, or one joined string if the parser wants a single value) — or as {"@type":"Place","name":…}. The names come from dataset.countries, as today. tests/ has the JSON-LD case to extend: assert the type is text/Place, never Country.

    Verify: after the deploy, Search Console → Datasets → the issue's Validate fix; the Rich Results test on https://permitrulebook.com/data/ shows no warning on spatialCoverage.

    Not s13 (in flight); a slice of its own, small, or folded into site #10's /data/ pass.

    Comments

    OytunOnal · 16 Sep 2026

    Live 2026-09-16 in de094a7 (s18): spatialCoverage is four {"@type":"Place","name":…} entries. Search Console's warning should leave the Datasets report on its next crawl; Validate fix is the human's click.

Next — v1.2.3 — the structure check reports no violation and no debt7 slices
  1. closed on
    23 Oct 2026

    #199 The carry has one entry and one level per file, a linter holds the shape limits, and structure.json merges without a conflict

    from #37real use #207real-green

    Spec of #37, from its grill of 2026-10-23 (rulings 1–16 on #37). Bump: internal, no ADR (ruling 16).

    Spec of #37, from its grill of 2026-10-23 (rulings 1–16 on #37). Bump: internal, no ADR (ruling 16).

    Problem Statement

    A maintainer of Permit Rulebook runs the structure check, and it is red on one line that no exception can cover: the carry's folder holds five files at its root, and a deep module has one entry. Four more exceptions on the carry fall due at v1.2.3:

    • three outward edges into its log wording;
    • its flow, which touches the build directory and the clock itself.

    CODING_STANDARDS.md names the shape limits, but "review reads them until a linter enforces them", and no linter exists. A 243-line function, nesting of 4 and a 1786-line test file all sat on master. In #110 three of the six hard Standards findings were these limits, and they drove the fix round's largest rewrite.

    docs/spine/structure.json is generated, yet in #167's build it conflicted in five merges. Each was settled by hand-regenerating the file, because no hand can resolve it correctly.

    Solution

    The carry.

    • It becomes a deep module with one entry, and every file in or around it says one level that matches the level's definition.
    • Its adapters live beside it, outside the module, and the command wires them.
    • The structure check shows no line for the carry.

    A linter.

    • ESLint holds the six shape limits, and its run is part of the green.
    • The file limit is lowered to 400 lines, and every line counts, comments and blank lines included.
    • What it flags today in the carry, plus every small hit, is fixed in this slice.
    • Every other length hit is an exception with its issue and its version. A test fails the green once the version comes.

    The merge rule.

    • A merge takes one side's structure.json without a conflict.
    • The structure command is rerun after any merge that changed it.

    User Stories

    1. As a maintainer, I want the structure check to show no root-files line for the carry, so that the check is green on structure for the first time since it was set up.
    2. As a maintainer, I want the carry's four v1.2.3 exceptions gone from dependency-rules.md, so that v1.2.3's release finds no debt due on the carry.
    3. As a maintainer, I want the carry's entry to be the flow and its ports, at application, so that reading the entry tells me what a carry does and what it needs from outside.
    4. As a maintainer, I want the live site, the build directory and the clock to be ports the entry names, so that the flow touches no disk, network or clock itself.
    5. As a maintainer, I want the build directory's adapter to be one small file of its own at infrastructure, so that every disk call the carry makes is in one place.
    6. As a maintainer, I want the clock handed over as Date.now by the command, so that no file exists only to wrap the clock.
    7. As a maintainer, I want the HTTP adapter outside the module at infrastructure, so that the command can wire it without reaching past the entry.
    8. As a maintainer, I want the command to stay main and be the one place the adapters are built and handed over, so that its level means what the definition says: wiring.
    9. As a maintainer, I want the log wording to be a domain file of its own outside the module, so that the module, the adapter and the command each read it inward, and the entry's interface stays about carrying.
    10. As a maintainer, I want the asset-name reading and the digest list in a private folder named generation, after the glossary's Generation, so that the folder's name says what its files describe.
    11. As a maintainer, I want the entry to re-export the three domain readers (readAssetNames, digestListOf, readAssetDigests) beside carryAssets, writeAssetDigests and ORIGIN_VAR, so that their own tests keep their seams and reach them only through the entry.
    12. As a maintainer, I want the carry's tests to pass an in-memory build directory and a fixed clock, as they already pass an in-memory live site, so that the flow's cases run without a disk and without time.
    13. As a maintainer, I want the build directory's adapter tested over a temporary folder, so that the real disk work is proven once, apart from the flow.
    14. As the deploy, I want the carry's outcome lines and its zero exit to be exactly what they are today, so that a deploy reads the same before and after the reshape.
    15. As a maintainer, I want the carry's files each at most 400 lines and their functions at most 40, so that the module this slice reshapes already meets the new limits.
    16. As a maintainer, I want npm run lint to run ESLint over src/, scripts/, tests/ and the .astro pages, so that one command answers whether the code keeps the shape limits.
    17. As a maintainer, I want a file over 400 lines to fail the lint, every line counted, so that length is not hidden in comments.
    18. As a maintainer, I want a function over 40 lines to fail the lint, every line counted, so that long functions are found by a machine, not by review.
    19. As a test author, I want a describe callback exempt from the 40-line function limit, so that grouping cases is never split apart for a number. Each it body and each helper is still held to 40.
    20. As a maintainer, I want nesting deeper than 3 to fail the lint, so that bodyWithin-style depth cannot land again.
    21. As a maintainer, I want more than 3 parameters to fail the lint, so that a long parameter list becomes an object.
    22. As a maintainer, I want a nested ternary to fail the lint, so that a plain if is written instead.
    23. As a maintainer, I want an unused name to fail the lint in .ts, .mjs and .astro alike, so that dead names do not sit on master.
    24. As a maintainer, I want the .astro pages' scripts linted under the same rules, so that the site's largest file is not the one file the limits never see.
    25. As a maintainer, I want the lint in the green, locally and in the deploy workflow beside npm test, so that a red lint stops a merge and a deploy the way a red test does.
    26. As a maintainer, I want CODING_STANDARDS.md's Commands to name the lint and the 400-line limit, so that a builder finds the command where every other command is.
    27. As a maintainer, I want every small hit fixed in this slice (10 nesting, 15 parameters, 7 nested ternaries, 3 unused names), so that only length hits, each with an issue, are left waiting.
    28. As a maintainer, I want every other length hit written as an exception, one entry per file and issue, naming the rules it may break, its issue and its version, so that the green stays at 0 problems while the debt is named.
    29. As a maintainer, I want a test that fails once package.json's version reaches an exception's until, naming the file and the issue, so that a lint debt cannot outlive its date, as the structure check's debts cannot.
    30. As a maintainer, I want the exceptions placed where the work already is (#56, #142, #127, #184, #182) and the rest in #198, so that each exception names an issue that will remove it.
    31. As the v1.2.3 re-plan, I want #182's and #198's exceptions dated 1.2.4, so that the re-plan has to place both before that release.
    32. As a maintainer merging two branches that each regenerated structure.json, I want the merge to take one side without a conflict, so that I never resolve a generated file by hand.
    33. As a maintainer, I want the merge driver set once in each clone by one command CODING_STANDARDS.md names (worktrees share it), so that the .gitattributes line works with nothing run on install. (Amended at the fix round, 2026-10-23.)
    34. As a maintainer, I want CODING_STANDARDS.md to say: after any merge that changed structure.json, rerun the structure command and commit the result, so that the merged tree's stamp is the tree's own.
    35. As a maintainer whose PR shows GitHub's conflict on structure.json, I want the documented way out (merge master locally, rerun, push), so that the GitHub button's limit is not a surprise.

    Implementation Decisions

    The carry (rulings 2–6):

    ModuleLevelHolds
    The command, carry-assetsmainreads its flags and the origin variable, builds the adapters, hands them and Date.now to the entry, prints, exits 0
    The live site over HTTPinfrastructuretoday's liveAnswerOverHttp and bodyWithin, moved out of the module beside the command
    The build directory on disk, build-directory-on-disk (new)infrastructurethe build-directory port over dist/: list the built assets with their bytes, say whether one is already built, write a carried asset, write the digest list
    The log wordingdomaintoday's wording helpers, moved out of the module beside the command
    The carry's entry, carry.mjsapplicationthe flow and its three ports: the live site (liveAnswerFor, as today), the build directory, the clock (now())
    generation/, privatedomainthe asset-name reading and the digest list
    • The entry's interface: carryAssets, writeAssetDigests, ORIGIN_VAR, plus the re-exported readAssetNames, digestListOf and readAssetDigests.
    • `writeAssetDigests` writes through the build-directory port.
    • `## Modules` still declares the carry's folder.
    • The four carry exceptions in dependency-rules.md are removed, and the structure command is rerun.
    • Unchanged behaviour: the outcome lines, their wording, the three outcomes and the zero exit stay as they are. The deploy workflow's comment pointing at the carry's header is updated to where that text now lives.

    The lint (rulings 7–14):

    • The tool: ESLint with a flat config at the root (// @level main, like the other root configs), typescript-eslint's parser with no type-aware rules, and eslint-plugin-astro for the pages' scripts. Each is pinned to an exact version.
    • The rules, all errors:
    • max-lines 400 and max-lines-per-function 40, neither skipping blank lines nor comments;
    • max-depth 3;
    • max-params 3;
    • no-nested-ternary;
    • the unused-names rule (@typescript-eslint/no-unused-vars for TypeScript, no-unused-vars otherwise).
    • The `describe` exemption: the config carries one small local rule. It runs the core max-lines-per-function and drops a report whose function is the argument of a describe call. Files under the tests pattern use it in place of the core rule.
    • `npm run lint`:
    • it lints src/, scripts/, tests/ and src/pages/**/*.astro, skipping what ## Ignore skips;
    • it joins the green in CODING_STANDARDS.md (Commands);
    • it runs as a step of the deploy workflow beside npm test;
    • the Commands' "Lint: none configured" line is replaced, with the file limit at 400.
    • The exception list: it lives in the config as one list, one entry per file and issue: { file, rules, issue, until } (a file with hits for two issues has two entries, each listing only the rules its issue removes; amended at #200's merge, 2026-10-23). Each entry turns those rules off for that file only. The list is also exported so the test can read it.
    • Where the exceptions point (ruling 14):
    IssueUntilFiles
    #561.2.3index.astro, question.ts, history.test.ts, s37.test.ts
    #1421.2.3route-page.ts; the files of countryPage, dataPage, datasetLd, feedbackPage, notFoundPage, siteHeader, siteFooter; route-page.test.ts, identity.test.ts
    #1271.2.3dev-server.mjs, render-assets.mjs
    #1841.2.4card-blocks.ts
    #1821.2.4browser.mjs
    #1981.2.4s13, first-paint, s27, s30, s23, s26, s25, orientation, s19, record test files, and every other test file with an it or helper over 40 lines

    An entry lists only the length rules its file breaks.

    • Fixed in this slice (ruling 12):
    • the carry's files and its two test files (carry-takes-attested-bytes-from-our-origin, live-site-over-http) under 400 lines, with their functions under 40;
    • every hit of max-depth, max-params, no-nested-ternary and the unused-names rule across the repository, including whatever the .astro script adds once it is linted.
    • No new comment rule: CODING_STANDARDS.md's comment line stays as it is (ruling 9). Spine brings its comment rules.

    The merge rule (ruling 15):

    • .gitattributes gains merge=ours for docs/spine/structure.json, beside its existing line-ending line.
    • The driver is set once per clone with git config merge.ours.driver true, the one command CODING_STANDARDS.md's Structure line names; worktrees share it, and CI never merges. No prepare script and no script of our own: at the fix round the human ruled (b) on PR #205's Security finding, so nothing runs on install and nothing writes under .git but the person who types the command (amended 2026-10-23; the earlier prepare design shipped in #202 and is removed).
    • CODING_STANDARDS.md's Structure line gains two things:
    • the rerun after any merge that changed the file;
    • the way out of GitHub's conflict: merge master into the branch locally, rerun, push. GitHub's merge ignores local drivers.

    Testing Decisions

    • A good test asserts what a seam does, not how; expected values come from the spec or a known literal, never recomputed the way the code does.
    • Seam 1: the carry's entry, `carryAssets` (existing).
    • The carry's suites pass an in-memory live site (today's helper), an in-memory build directory (a new helper beside it) and a fixed clock.
    • Every case they hold today keeps its id and its outcome.
    • The domain readers' cases (asset-names, digest-list, and readAssetNames in pipeline) import through the entry.
    • The pipeline test's reading of the deploy workflow stays as it is.
    • Prior art: tests/carry-assets/.
    • Seam 2: the adapters.
    • The HTTP adapter's suite keeps its cases over a local server and imports the adapter where it now lives.
    • The build directory's adapter gets a few cases over a temporary folder: list, already built, write an asset, write the digest list.
    • Seam 3: the lint config, through ESLint's own API (new).
    • For each rule, one source text that breaks it and one that keeps it, linted under the repository's config.
    • The cases include the describe exemption (a long describe passes, a long it inside it fails) and one .astro script.
    • In the same suite, the exception list is checked against package.json's version: it fails once the version reaches an entry's until, naming the file and the issue. Its red path is proven with a version past an entry.
    • A case also checks that each entry still names a file the lint would otherwise flag, so a fixed file's entry cannot linger.
    • The merge rule is not a test. It is proven once and shown in the PR: two branches that each regenerated structure.json merge without a conflict, and the rerun stamps the merged tree (ruling 15).
    • Moves keep case ids: a file split or moved keeps its case ids and outcomes, compared before and after.
    • The structure check shows no line for the carry, and no root-files line, on the PR's tree.

    Out of Scope

    • structure.mjs --root (Spine #373) and Spine's own structure.json merge rule (Spine #280).
    • The length hits placed in #56, #142, #127, #184, #182 and #198: here they are only written as exceptions.
    • A rule on comments (ruling 9).
    • Type-aware lint rules, a formatter, and the data repository's lint.

    Further Notes

    • Real use:
    • the first deploy after the merge prints the carry's outcome lines as before, and publishes the digest list;
    • npm run lint is green on master;
    • the structure check shows no line for the carry.
    • Grill record: rulings 1–16 are comments on #37, written as each was made.

    Structure

    • module scripts/carry-assets @application — the carry: the flow and its ports (the live site, the build directory, the clock); its entry turns from application and infrastructure to application
    • module scripts/live-site-over-http.mjs @infrastructure — the live site over HTTP, moved out of the carry
    • module scripts/build-directory-on-disk.mjs @infrastructure — the build directory over dist/
    • module scripts/log-wording.mjs @domain — the carry's log wording, moved out of the carry
    • module eslint.config.mjs @main — the lint's rules, its local describe rule and its exception list
    • edge scripts/carry-assets.mjs → scripts/carry-assets — dependency
    • edge scripts/carry-assets.mjs → scripts/live-site-over-http.mjs — dependency
    • edge scripts/carry-assets.mjs → scripts/build-directory-on-disk.mjs — dependency
    • edge scripts/carry-assets.mjs → scripts/log-wording.mjs — dependency
    • edge scripts/carry-assets → scripts/log-wording.mjs — dependency
    • edge scripts/live-site-over-http.mjs → scripts/log-wording.mjs — dependency
    • seam scripts/carry-assets — production: the live site over HTTP, the build directory on disk, Date.now; test: the live site in memory, the build directory in memory, a fixed clock

    Tickets

    • #201 The carry: one entry, one level per file
    • #202 structure.json merges without a conflict
    • #203 The small shape hits are fixed
