    #208 The interview page is wiring alone: the interview, its history and the country search are deep modules, the arrival is one call, and every Back says where it goes

    from #56real use #217real-green

    Spec of #56, from its grill of 2026-10-23 (rulings 1–12 on #56). Bump: Z, internal, with P7, P8/P9 and F11 as fixes. No ADR (ruling 1).

    Spec of #56, from its grill of 2026-10-23 (rulings 1–12 on #56). Bump: Z, internal, with P7, P8/P9 and F11 as fixes. No ADR (ruling 1).

    Problem Statement

    A maintainer of Permit Rulebook opens the interview page and finds the whole interview in it. index.astro is 1,387 lines:

    • a 1,030-line bundled script holds the screens' state in a dozen lets, the browser's history, the arrival, the country search's glue, the landing and the beat;
    • a 204-line style block follows it.

    Two exceptions fall due. The structure check carries two mixed-levels exceptions due at v1.2.4: the page (presentation infrastructure main) and scope.ts (application presentation). Four lint exceptions on #56 fail the green at v1.2.3:

    • the page over 400 lines, with functions over 40;
    • questionCardHtml at 79 lines;
    • history.test.ts at 566 lines and s37.test.ts at 639.

    The interview's real failures were found only in Chrome. Another tab grew or shrank the record; an outside entrance sent Back off the site; the arrival moved France three times. The decisions sit in the page's lets and ifs, where no Node test reaches them.

    A reader meets four small faults:

    • P7: on an edit screen, "← Back" and "Keep this answer" sit side by side, and nothing says which goes where.
    • P8/P9: Start over opens question one mid-page, with the hero and the stamp above the fold.
    • F11: from /germany/'s "Check yours" with a record on file, the verdict comes back with nothing saying it is from earlier.
    • F11: Start over there asks "Where are you looking to go?" while the address still says Germany.

    Solution

    For the maintainer:

    • The page becomes wiring alone, about 170 lines at one level, main. It builds the browser's history adapter, hands it and the record's store to the interview, and starts it.
    • The interview is a deep module. Every decision it makes comes from one pure step function, tested in Node.
    • The browser's history is a deep module of its own behind a port, so s37's two-tab corners run in Node with a fake browser history.
    • The country search is a deep module whose controller holds no DOM.
    • The arrival is one call.
    • Both mixed-levels exceptions and all four of #56's lint exceptions go.

    For the reader:

    • Every Back says where it goes, and so does Keep this answer.
    • Start over lands at the top of the page like a first visit.
    • A verdict brought back from a kept record says it is continuing from earlier.
    • Start over on a country's or a route's address starts over in that country.

    User Stories

    1. As a maintainer, I want index.astro at most 400 lines and tagged main alone, so that the page's one job is to build the adapters and start the interview.
    2. As a maintainer, I want the page never to call window.history or touch the screen list, so that the browser's history has one owner.
    3. As a maintainer, I want the page's style block in a stylesheet of the interview module, imported as the verdict's is, so that 204 lines of CSS do not count against the page.
    4. As a maintainer, I want the page's script to be a few lines that call startInterview, so that the bundled module is the interview module's entry, not the page.
    5. As a maintainer, I want the record's store handed to the interview by the page, inside today's guard for blocked site data, so that localStorage is reached in one place.
    6. As a maintainer, I want the interview to be a deep module declared in ## Modules, so that the structure check holds what is private to it.
    7. As a maintainer, I want its entry, startInterview(ports), at main, wiring its own parts, so that its level is what the definition says a deep module's wiring entry is.
    8. As a maintainer, I want every decision the interview makes to come from one pure step function, interviewAfter, at application, so that the decisions live in one place a test can reach.
    9. As a test author, I want interviewAfter re-exported by the entry with its types, so that the tests reach the decisions through the entry, as the verdict's tests reach verdictOf.
    10. As a maintainer, I want the interview's drawing parts (painting, landing, focus, the beat, the ledger's wiring) at presentation, only applying what the step function returned, so that no decision hides in the DOM code.
    11. As a maintainer, I want each gesture named (loaded, answered, edited a row, back, kept, restarted, the browser moved), so that the step function's input is the list of things a reader can do.
    12. As a maintainer, I want the step function's result to say the next state, the screen, the lines, the landing, the beat, the save and the history move, so that the page's former lets become one value.
    13. As a maintainer, I want the screen transitions of today's screenAfter folded into interviewAfter, so that the masthead's one-writer rule (B4) is decided where every other transition is.
    14. As a maintainer, I want the interview's history to be a deep module of its own, declared in ## Modules, so that its rule can be tested without a browser.
    15. As a maintainer, I want its entry at application to declare a BrowserHistory port (read the state, push, replace, go back, listen for moves), so that the browser is an adapter and a test can be another.
    16. As a maintainer, I want the browser's adapter for that port to be one small infrastructure file beside the page, so that every history call the site makes is in one file.
    17. As a maintainer, I want the nine history exports to leave screen.ts for the history module's private domain parts, so that the screen list and the held-step rule have one home.
    18. As a maintainer, I want the restoring flag, the entry the page opened onto, the first held step and the rebuild on load inside the history module, so that the page and the interview never see them.
    19. As a maintainer, I want the history to tell me, before a click, where the screen's Back will go, so that its label and its move cannot disagree.
    20. As a test author, I want another tab growing the record, shrinking it, or starting over, tested in Node through the history's entry, so that s37's two-tab corners no longer need Chrome.
    21. As a test author, I want an outside entrance, a re-entrance and an entry written by the build before s37 tested in Node, so that Back never leaves the site where it should stay, and never stays where it should leave.
    22. As a maintainer, I want the country search to be a deep module declared in ## Modules, so that one control lives in one folder instead of three files.
    23. As a maintainer, I want the search's controller (countrySearchOf) to hold no DOM, so that typing, the keys, Enter and a click run in Node.
    24. As a reader using the passport search, I want Enter to commit the highlighted row and nothing else, proven on the glue as well as the state, so that the wrong nationality is never recorded in silence again (B1).
    25. As a reader typing in an IME, I want its committing Enter left to the IME, so that composing a name does not answer the question.
    26. As a maintainer, I want the search's DOM mount to be a thin file of three listeners, so that what only Chrome can check is small.
    27. As a maintainer, I want listbox.ts, the rows' markup and the class hint private to the search, so that nothing else depends on the control's insides.
    28. As a maintainer, I want every sentence of the search in the copy module, so that the page and question.ts type none.
    29. As a maintainer, I want the arrival to be one call, arrivalOf, returning the record after the move, the route that sorts first and the facts of the arrival line, so that the page's seven arrival lets become one value.
    30. As a maintainer, I want scope.ts at application alone, with the line's words and markup in a presentation file of their own, so that its mixed-levels exception goes.
    31. As a test author, I want B2 and "France three times" tested through arrivalOf with the address given as a string, so that the arrival is tested in Node, not in Chrome alone.
    32. As a reader on an edit screen, I want "← Back" to name the question it goes to, so that I know it steps back one question.
    33. As a reader on an edit screen, I want "Keep this answer" to name the next question, or say it shows my result, so that I know where it goes.
    34. As a reader on any question screen, I want "← Back" to name the question it goes to, so that Back reads the same everywhere.
    35. As a reader who pressed Start over, I want the page at its top, header, hero and stamp in view, so that it feels like starting again, not like being dropped mid-page.
    36. As a keyboard reader who pressed Start over, I want the focus on the first control without a second scroll, so that I do not tab through the header to begin.
    37. As a reader who pressed Start over, I want no beat on the first question, so that nothing refuses my first click on a screen I did not reach by answering.
    38. As a returning reader whose kept record opens on the verdict, I want the line "Continuing where you left off — N answers kept." with Start over, so that I know this verdict is from my earlier answers.
    39. As a returning reader sent by a country page, I want one line on my first screen, not two, so that the arrival and the return are not said twice.
    40. As a reader who answers, edits or goes back, I want no resumed line on the screens that follow, so that the line marks only the return.
    41. As a reader on /?country=de who presses Start over, I want my answers cleared and Germany put back on the record, so that the screen matches the address I am on and the button I pressed.
    42. As a reader on /?route=… who presses Start over, I want that route to sort first in my next verdict again, so that Start over does not lose what I came for.
    43. As a reader on a shared computer, I want Start over to clear the stored record wherever the address points, so that the next person never sees my salary.
    44. As a reader on a plain /, I want Start over to behave exactly as today, so that nothing changes for a reader who came with no link.
    45. As a maintainer, I want questionCardHtml split into three parts of at most 40 lines each (the lines above the question, the options, the exits), so that the question card meets the lint without an exception.
    46. As a maintainer, I want screen.ts left with only the Screen and Masthead types the verdict and the question card share, so that no flow logic sits beside them.
    47. As a maintainer, I want history.test.ts and s37.test.ts each at most 400 lines, every it and helper at most 40, so that #56's lint exceptions on them go.
    48. As a maintainer, I want the page's typed words ("You declared", "Start over", the description, the title) in the copy module, so that the page obeys the copy rule.
    49. As a maintainer, I want the two dev-only hooks (?dev-profile=, ?dev-probe=) deleted, so that no dead code moves into the new module.
    50. As a maintainer, I want the structure check to show no line for index.astro or scope.ts, and dependency-rules.md to lose both exceptions, so that v1.2.4's due debt is cleared a milestone early.
    51. As a maintainer, I want every #56 entry gone from the lint's exception list, so that v1.2.3's release finds no lint debt of #56's.
    52. As a reader, I want every screen, answer, edit, Back, reload and arrival to behave as before, except for P7, P8/P9 and F11, so that a reshape of this size changes nothing else I see.

    Implementation Decisions

    The page (ruling 2):

    • index.astro is tagged main.
    • Its frontmatter draws the first paint by calling presentation functions, as today.
    • Its script:
    • builds the browser-history adapter and the record's store (localStorage inside today's try/catch);
    • calls startInterview with them.
    • Its words move to the copy module: the frame file or the interview file, by who reads them.
    • The two dev-only hooks are deleted (ruling 12).

    The interview module, `interview` (rulings 2, 10, 11): a new deep module, declared in ## Modules.

    PartLevelHolds
    The entrymainstartInterview(ports): wires the parts; re-exports interviewAfter and its types
    flow/, privateapplicationinterviewAfter(dataset, state, gesture), the interview's state, the gestures, and what was screenAfter
    The drawing parts, privatepresentationpainting (draw, paint, say), the landing, the focus, the beat, the ledger's wiring, the verdict's paint
    Its stylesheet—the page's former style block, imported by the page as the verdict's stylesheet is
    • The step function's input: the dataset, the state, and one gesture:
    • loaded (the kept record and the address's search);
    • answered (value, and the label the next screen says was recorded);
    • editedRow (field);
    • back (the field the history said to show in place);
    • kept;
    • restarted (the address's search);
    • browserMoved (field, or the verdict).
    • The step function's result:
    • the next state (answers, asked order, the field being edited, the arrival);
    • the screen (a question's field, or the verdict);
    • the lines (arrival, resumed, set aside, just recorded);
    • the landing (for example card under the header, page top, none);
    • whether the beat runs;
    • whether the record is saved;
    • the history move to hand the history module.
    • Wiring: the entry hands the history move to the history module and the save to the record's store. The drawing parts apply the rest.

    The history module, `interview-history` (ruling 3): a new deep module, declared in ## Modules.

    • Its entry is application and declares the BrowserHistory port: read the current entry's state, push, replace, go back, listen for moves.
    • `openInterviewHistory(browser, { asked, showing })` returns:
    • pushScreen(field) and replaceScreen(field) (renamed from movedForward and redrawnInPlace at the fix round, ruling 3): a screen was drawn by answering (a new entry) or by any other gesture (the entry it stands on). The field is null for the verdict.
    • startOver() (renamed from restarted(), ruling 3): the entries go with the record.
    • Back skips entries that show the question already on screen, and backTarget names the question before them; the port's back takes a step count (fix round, ruling 1).
    • backTarget(asked, editing): the field the screen's Back goes to.
    • back(asked, editing): either "browser" (it asked the browser to go back), or the field to show in place.
    • onMove(show): the browser moved; the field to draw, or the verdict.
    • Its private `domain` parts: the screen list, the held-step rule, and reading an entry's state. They are the nine exports that leave screen.ts.
    • Kept inside: the restoring flag, the entry the page opened onto, the first held step and the rebuild on load.
    • Amended against the standards' "no boolean argument that switches what a function does": ruling 3's drawn(field, advanced) is written as two functions.
    • The adapter: the browser's adapter is an infrastructure file beside the page over window.history.

    The country search, `country-search` (ruling 5): a new deep module, presentation, declared in ## Modules.

    • Its entry:
    • countrySearchHtml(question, declared): the box and the empty list, out of the question card.
    • countrySearchOf(options, { chosen, classed }, onCommit) → { typeQuery(value), pressKey({ key, isComposing }), clickRow(value) } (renamed at the fix round, ruling 3). typeQuery returns a view ({ listHtml, status, activeRowId, expanded }), pressKey takes the key event's two named fields (amended against the standards' boolean-argument rule; ruling 5 wrote key(key, isComposing)) and returns whether it handled the key and the view, and Enter and a click call onCommit(value, label). It holds no DOM.
    • mountCountrySearch(root, search): three listeners that apply a view.
    • Private: the listbox state machine, the rows' markup, the class hint.
    • Copy: the page's three search sentences and question.ts' two move to the interview's copy file.

    The arrival (rulings 4, 8):

    • scope.ts is tagged application.
    • `arrivalOf(dataset, search, record)` → { record, scopedRouteId, line }.
    • line is the arrival's facts (the route, the country, rescoped, kept, asks), or null when nothing moved.
    • replayRecord and scopedFirst stay exported. arrivalFrom, countryArrivalFrom, destinationFor, destinationForCountry and arrivalPlan become private.
    • `arrivalLineHtml(dataset, facts, glossary)`, a new presentation file, holds the arrival sentence, the question phrase and the line's markup. It glosses the route's name with the screen's glossary.

    What the reader sees:

    • P7 (ruling 6):
    • every question screen's Back reads "← Back to <short label>", where the target is backTarget;
    • Keep reads "Keep this answer — next: <short label>", or "Keep this answer — see your result" when nothing is left;
    • the words are three entries of the interview's copy file;
    • the short label is the dataset's, the ledger's own.
    • F11, first half (ruling 7):
    • a verdict that is the first screen of a return onto a kept record carries the resumed line, with the ledger's Start over moved in, where the arrival line sits;
    • it is never beside an arrival line, and never after a gesture;
    • the record's format is unchanged.
    • F11, second half (ruling 8): Start over clears the record and the history, then runs arrivalOf on an empty record with the address's search, the same path the load takes. On a plain / this is today's Start over. The device keeps nothing after Start over on any address: the arrival's destination lives on screen until the next answer saves the record (fix round, ruling 2, after the Security axis's L1).
    • P8/P9 (ruling 9):
    • Start over's landing is the page's top on every width: smooth, or instant under reduced motion;
    • the focus goes to the first control without a second scroll;
    • no beat runs.

    Around the module (ruling 11):

    • screen.ts keeps only the shared screen and masthead types.
    • question.ts stays outside. questionCardHtml is split into three parts of at most 40 lines: the lines above the question, the options, the exits.
    • record.ts, first-paint.ts and gloss.ts are unchanged.

    The structure and the lint:

    • ## Modules gains interview, interview-history and country-search.
    • The exceptions for scope.ts and index.astro are removed from dependency-rules.md, and the structure command is rerun.
    • #56's four entries leave the lint's exception list.

    Testing Decisions

    • A good test asserts what a seam decides, not how, and not the words. The expected values come from this spec, the rulings or a known literal, never recomputed the way the code computes them.
    • Seam 1: `interviewAfter`, through the interview's entry (new).
    • Every gesture's screen, lines, landing, beat, save and history move.
    • P7's targets: Back's field from the history, and Keep's field or the result.
    • The resumed line on a returned-to question and verdict, and its absence after a gesture and beside an arrival line.
    • Start over arriving again on ?country= and ?route=, and today's Start over on /.
    • Start over landing at the page top with no beat.
    • B4's property walk, moved from screen.test.ts.
    • Prior art: screen.test.ts, the verdict's verdictOf cases.
    • Seam 2: `openInterviewHistory`, with an in-memory `BrowserHistory` (new).
    • Push and replace, Back past and on the first held step, backTarget, Start over.
    • An outside entrance, a re-entrance, an entry written before s37.
    • Another tab growing the record, shrinking it, and starting over and answering again.
    • Prior art: history.test.ts, rewritten through the entry, and the corners of s37.test.ts.
    • **S

    #149 Each repository's commands file, so Spine's red-before and same-cases run there

    real use #226mock-green

    Repositories: both, the site and OytunOnal/permit-rulebook-data.

    Repositories: both, the site and OytunOnal/permit-rulebook-data.

    From the retro of #133 (comment on #97, 2026-10-21), seen before in #86's; opened on the human's word.

    What the builders hit

    Spine's briefing asks each builder to show a new test red on the base commit with "the project's command that runs today's tests on an older commit". Neither repository has one; Spine's own red-before.py runs Python suites only. So in #133 every builder did it by hand: a scratch copy of the test pointed at the base commit's dataset, with its own config. It cost each one the same frictions:

    • tar -xf … -C C:/… fails under Git Bash (C: read as a host) until --force-local, in three builders;
    • the auto-mode classifier refused cp base-dataset.json data/dataset.json as an irreversible local destruction;
    • on the site, npm run build runs astro check over tests/, so a red-first case that names a missing export breaks the build the red needs (#137's and #139's builders).

    And #139's builder skipped it: "I did not run them on the base, so 'red there too' is reasoned, not measured."

    Done when

    • Each repository has npm run red-before -- <commit> <test file> [...]: it runs today's named test files against the code and data of <commit> in a scratch copy outside the tree, and reports each case red or green there. It changes nothing in the working tree.
    • On the site it builds what the tests need without type-checking tests/, so a case naming a missing export still runs red.
    • It is named in CODING_STANDARDS.md's commands, where Spine's briefing looks for the project's red-before command.
    • Proven on a known case: s14's old "over the shipped state" case, run against 2310103 with today's state, reports red.

    Rescoped at the retro of #208 (the human, 2026-10-23)

    Spine now ships red-before and same-cases for any project:

    • node <spine>/scripts/red-before.mjs <commit> <test file> ... runs today's named test files on the commit's code;
    • node <spine>/scripts/same-cases.mjs <commit> [test file ...] says whether a change kept every case's outcome.

    Both read the project's docs/spine/commands.json (beforeTests, runTests with {files}, tests). Spine's briefing points builders at them. So this issue no longer builds a command; it gives each repository that file.

    In #208 every builder still proved red-first by hand, and five scripts in four agents compared case ids before and after a move.

    Done when (replaces the list above):

    • Each repository has docs/spine/commands.json, so that red-before.mjs and same-cases.mjs run there.
    • On the site, beforeTests builds what the tests need without type-checking tests/, so a case naming a missing export still runs red.
    • Each is proven on a known case: s14's old "over the shipped state" case, run against 2310103 with today's state, reports red.
    • CODING_STANDARDS.md's Commands name the file.

    Rescoped again: Spine's whole setup (the human, 2026-10-24)

    After PR #219, the human ruled that #149 grows into Spine's setup slice and runs now, before #127: "evet önce spine setup ı". It follows Spine's skills/slice/references/project-tools.md whole, in this repository:

    1. docs/spine/commands.json, from the commands the site already runs.
    2. Spine's lint rules: copy-lint-rules.mjs copies them into spine-lint/, loaded as the preset in place of eslint.config.mjs's own shape limits. That adds the named-condition rule, spine/constant-reason, and the function limit on a describe callback.
    3. Ceilings in docs/spine/ceilings.json for the files over 400 lines.
    4. Every other breach recorded as Suppressions (npx eslint --suppress-all .), the constant-reason breaches included (Spine v2.6.0, step 5).
    5. The structure check in the green.
    6. .run/ in .gitignore, which is already there.
    7. CODING_STANDARDS.md's Commands as Spine's skeleton has them.
    8. node <spine>/scripts/green.mjs ends on green: stamped <tree>.

    The data repository gets its docs/spine/commands.json, as the rescope above says. Its lint stays #166's.

    Done when, adding to the list above:

    • every step of project-tools.md is done in the site;
    • Spine's green stamps both repositories' trees.

  6. #183 The verdict's reckoning carries its own level: in-view.ts and steps.ts say application

    From the architecture read at v1.2.2's close (card 2, strong), placed on the human's word, 2026-10-23.

    From the architecture read at v1.2.2's close (card 2, strong), placed on the human's word, 2026-10-23.

    What a maintainer sees

    Every file of src/lib/verdict/compose/ is tagged presentation, so the structure check cannot see an edge from the verdict's reckoning to its words. #170's "verdictOf returns the screen as data" has no level to stand on.

    • compose/in-view.ts is application code, tagged presentation. It holds resultsInViewOf (evaluate, notices, isClosed), groupedRoutesOf and Compared, with no word and no markup. Its one outward edge is scopedFirst in src/lib/scope.ts, a four-line stable sort called only here and by tests/s6-edges.test.ts.
    • compose/steps.ts holds two levels:
    • application: arrangeSteps, isLadder, nearestStep, arrangedRowsOf, arrangedLineIn, offerLineIn, routesAnOfferFreesFor;
    • presentation: seekHintHtml and the orAbove title.

    The proposed fix

    • in-view.ts is tagged // @level application. scopedFirst moves into it, and tests/s6-edges.test.ts reads it through the verdict's entry; or it waits for #56 to tag scope.ts application.
    • steps.ts is split:
    • seekHintHtml moves to html.ts beside the steps heading, with isExploring exported;
    • orAbove is applied where the title is printed (unlockCardHtml in cards.ts, the headline's nearest), from step.folded.length;
    • what remains is // @level application.
    • dependency-rules.md's level list does not change, and the verdict's entry stays presentation.

    Before a milestone takes it

    It moves two files from presentation to application, so an ADR records the move first (slice, "When the milestone's last slice lands", step 2).

  7. #224 Every browser case waits on an event, never a fixed time, and passes on a busy runner

    Found: the data dispatch's deploy, run 40082762975 (2026-10-24 09:38), failed its Test step on two browser cases. Today's page was not published (#140). The same code passed all 1211 cases on a local machine twice the same day. - tests/s37.test.ts, "mid-interview, the screen's Back and the browser's land on the same question": expected [ Array(3) ] to deeply equal [ Array(3) ]. The two walks' live questions differed. - tests/first-paint.test.ts, "the page as it first paints is already the page (s10)", 1280x800, /?country=fr:

    Found: the data dispatch's deploy, run 40082762975 (2026-10-24 09:38), failed its Test step on two browser cases. Today's page was not published (#140). The same code passed all 1211 cases on a local machine twice the same day.

    • tests/s37.test.ts, "mid-interview, the screen's Back and the browser's land on the same question": expected [ Array(3) ] to deeply equal [ Array(3) ]. The two walks' live questions differed.
    • tests/first-paint.test.ts, "the page as it first paints is already the page (s10)", 1280x800, /?country=fr: CLS 0.0504 — [#main x+272px] · footer had no room at first paint · subline 94px · ledger open, where 0 is expected.

    The promise: SPINE_STANDARDS.md (Tests): "A test waits on an event, never on a sleep. A timing test sets its limit from the work it times …, never a fixed number of seconds."

    The mechanism, for s37: the walker's act makes a gesture, then sleeps a fixed ON_PAGE_MS = 450 before reading the tab (tests/s37.test.ts:86,130). The number was sized on a quiet local machine: "about 32 times the 14 ms an answer … took to draw … alone on the machine". On a busy CI runner, the tab is read before it has drawn. Across the browser cases, fixed waits stand in 17 files (about 106 lines: settle(…), *_SETTLE_MS, goto(url, settleMs) in scripts/browser.mjs).

    For first-paint: not reproduced yet. A shift of 0.05 on #main with "footer had no room at first paint" may be a real layout race on a slow machine, not only a slow test. That would break the first paint's promise (#66, #71). The diagnosis decides which.

    Precedent: #215's merger had no single green full run because of an s27 and an s37 flake; the cause was the walker's fixed 1,200 ms wait, which #208's fix round replaced by an event. This is the third time a fixed wait has turned a browser case red.

    Done when

    • No browser case waits a fixed time: each waits on the event it reads, such as a draw, a history entry or a document change, with a limit only as a backstop.
    • The first-paint shift is reproduced under a slowed CPU and judged: a test's race, or the page's. A fix to the page, if needed, comes with its case.
    • The browser cases pass under a slowed CPU (Chrome's CPU throttling) as they pass on a quiet machine.

    Comments

    OytunOnal · 24 Oct 2026

    Another instance, 2026-10-24, the local green on #223's branch (zod-audit, a lockfile-only change).

    tests/orientation.test.ts:381, "every kind of page loads, renders and opens its menu with no CSP violation": the menu did not open, Expected: "true", Received: "no button".   1. #51 A read's failure is judged in one place

    Repository: OytunOnal/permit-rulebook-data (the watch).

    Repository: OytunOnal/permit-rulebook-data (the watch).

    Problem. The step from what went wrong to {error, failure} happens at each throw site, in both tiers: src/watch/fetch-source.ts 485–756 (afterAnswer, fetchSource), browser.ts 110–190 (its catch), cdp.ts 97–100 (servedFailure) and 380–546 (about eight throw new ReadFailure), core.ts 387–389 (processingFailure) and 450–500 (judge). printableWithin is called 12 times in cdp.ts and 11 in fetch-source.ts, plus request.ts and steps.ts. The empty-body floor is judged twice (fetchSource, then judge). The browser's catch cannot be reached through an interface, so tests/s36.test.ts:651–666 pins it by copying its expression, and browser.ts asks whoever edits it to edit that case too. failure.ts (15 exports, among them constants like MOST_OF_A_PAGE_WORD, NOTE_BEFORE, noteOfLength) is a toolkit whose interface is nearly as wide as its implementation.

    Deepening. Readers report the raw facts — who said no, what was said, the HTTP status, whether a body arrived — and one module turns them into the failure class (transient, refused-by-source, refused-by-us), the bounded printable sentence, the after HTTP n prefix and the empty-body floor, returning the one report shape core reads.

    Wins. A rule like s36's a page's words stay the page's becomes a one-file change; the class and sentence rules are tested once, as a table; a fake CDP Session adapter can drive the browser's catch without Chrome (today 10 describe.skipIf(noChrome) blocks need a real browser).

    Evidence. 6339635 touched 7 watch files, 36405de 6, a012920 4, d09f875 3.

    Done when no reader builds a failure itself, the copied-expression case in s36.test.ts is replaced by a case through the module, and the failure rules have one table test.

    #82 — core.ts reads as a binary file: a raw NUL in sliceFingerprint

    Repository: permit-rulebook-data.

    Problem. src/watch/core.ts (about 1260 lines) has a raw U+0000 byte at sliceFingerprint:

    sha256(`${entry.slice.from}<NUL>${entry.slice.to}`)

    A NUL is a good separator, since no slice text can contain one. But because the byte is raw, ripgrep treats the whole file as binary. rg cutExcerpt src/watch finds the caller in run-says.ts but not the definition, and the architecture review's own searches missed it. Any search of the watch's largest file needs grep -a.

    Proposed fix (architecture review at the close of v1.2.0, card 4, "Strong"):

    • Write the separator as the escape \0 (${entry.slice.from}\0${entry.slice.to}), with a comment saying why it is a NUL.
    • The escape yields the same character, so every slice_read fingerprint in watch/state.json stays byte-identical, and no re-baseline follows.

    Done when:

    • core.ts holds no raw control byte, and ripgrep searches it as text.
    • A case shows that a fingerprint taken before the change is unchanged.

    One character; it fits in any pull request on the data repository.

    Line as of data `34c358d`: `src/watch/core.ts:286`.

  5. #179 The job offer is decided once, in the data package, and the site's offer line reads it

    Found by #167's fix round (outside its list) and ruled "later" by the human on 2026-10-22, on PR #178.

    Found by #167's fix round (outside its list) and ruled "later" by the human on 2026-10-22, on PR #178.

    Repositories: both.

    What the code says twice

    The data package decides which answer of the situation question is the job offer in isJobOffer (src/offer-frees.ts), and counts the routes an offer would free with it. The predicate is not exported. So the site's offer line, offerLineIn (src/lib/verdict/compose/steps.ts), picks the answer it names with the same condition typed again: !o.is_arranged && !o.is_fallback && !o.is_unknown.

    Today the two agree, so the reader sees nothing wrong. If the dataset gains another situation answer, the two could disagree. The offer line would then name one answer while counting the routes another one frees.

    Done when

    • The data package exports the job offer from its public entry, and the site's offer line reads it from there. No condition picking the offer is typed in the site.
    • A case at the data's public entry decides which option is the job offer; the site's seam-1 offer-line case still passes unchanged.
    • data.lock is pinned to the data commit that exports it.

    #81 — The stand-in belongs to its cover

    Repository: this one (the site).

    Problem. The glossary defines a stand-in as the line shown in the covered place until the cover comes off. Since #67 the cover has one owner (src/lib/first-paint.ts), but its stand-in still has four:

    • COVERS knows the stand-in only as a class name (uncovers.standIn), and STAND_IN_RULES styles it.
    • src/lib/question.ts (renderStandInsOverQuestionOne, holdLineHtml) writes its markup and spells the same class names again.
    • src/lib/copy.ts holds its words (RETURNING_LINE, LINK_ARRIVAL_LINE).
    • src/pages/index.astro paints the stand-ins, removes them with querySelectorAll(".stand-in"), and styles .stand-in-verdict in its own <style>.

    The class name is the seam, and it is spelled by hand on both sides. A test on the rendered string is what keeps the four spellings equal, and a fourth cover takes four edits.

    Proposed fix (architecture review at the close of v1.2.0, card 2, "Worth exploring"):

    • COVERS carries each stand-in's line.
    • first-paint.ts renders the stand-ins from the set (markup and stylesheet both). takeCoversOff removes cover and stand-ins in the one task that draws the reader's first screen.
    • question.ts draws question one and nothing of the first paint.
    • index.astro loses its .stand-in selector and its .stand-in-verdict style.

    Done when:

    • A new cover with its stand-in is one entry in the set.
    • The pairing of cover and stand-in is a property of the set, tested through it, not through a rendered string.
    • No first-paint behaviour moves: every arrival still holds still.

    Lines are as of site `4136be3`: `src/lib/question.ts` 188–206; `src/pages/index.astro` 67 and 122 (render), 245 (removal), 1787 (style). Report: `.run/architecture-review-2026-10-18-v1.2.0.html` (git-ignored).

    Which figure a date belongs to can still be told from the order, so #79's check 3 passed on its words. The date's weight is the defect: it competes with the amount it dates.

    Why

    headlineFigures (src/lib/route-page.ts) puts the <time> after the answer-label span, inside p.answer-figure. That rule sets font: 600 1.5rem var(--font-mono), and nothing styles the time, so it inherits the figure's size. Before #76 the panel's date sat in the small sentence under the figures.

    Done when

    • A figure's read date is set like its label (the label's size and colour from tokens.css), on the same line as the label where it fits.
    • The separator never sits alone at a line's end: it stays with the date it introduces.
    • At 390 and at 1920, on a route with one figure, two figures and points, the amount is the only large thing in the panel.
    • The first paint still holds still, and every tap target is still 44 px at 390.

    #85 later rewrites what the date says ("first read … · confirmed unchanged …"). This issue is only how large it is, so it does not wait for #85.

    Folds in

    #58 — A page shell, and the two import cycles go with it

    Repository: this one (the site).

    Problem. Six documents are assembled by hand — src/lib/route-page.ts 700–811, country-page.ts 167–262, data-page.ts 84–260 and 273–315, feedback-page.ts ~100–199, not-found.ts 50–95, and src/pages/index.astro 81–140 — each repeating the doctype, contentSecurityPolicy(), iconLinks(), the head meta, siteHeader(navCountries(dataset)), siteFooter(…, footerFacts(…)), MENU_SCRIPT and the beacon. The shared parts live in whichever page grew them first: PAGE_CSS in route-page.ts (imported by data-page, feedback-page, not-found), footerFacts and navCountries in country-page.ts (imported by five modules). So pages import each other, and two pairs are import cycles today: country-page ↔ route-page and data-page ↔ dataset-ld. One concept, the provenance line (quote, host, read day, official link), has two implementations — card.ts provenanceHtml (387) and route-page.ts quoteBlock (278) — under the same class names .src and .rail, styled differently in two stylesheets (index.astro 2090/2123, route-page.ts 894/910).

    Deepening. One page-shell module returns the full document for a place (which page, its title, its extra head) and owns the shared CSS, the header, the footer facts and the policy; each page supplies only its body.

    Wins. A header, footer or CSP change touches one module; the two cycles go, which the structure check (#37) would otherwise report first; identity.test, footer.test and csp.test can test the shell once instead of reading every built page.

    Evidence. f8737da, d77471d and cb9d042 each touched six page files; f6b9a37 rewrote contentSecurityPolicy([MENU_SCRIPT]) in every shell.

    Done when no page imports another page, every document comes from the shell, and the provenance line has one implementation.

    Raised by the architecture review of 2026-10-17 (`/improve-codebase-architecture`, scoped by the last 300 commits of both repositories); filed on the human's word to take every candidate. Line numbers are as of site `9f4d6c8` and data `1cb26e8`.

    Folds in

  2. #98 Every file the data package reads has a schema and bounds

    From the Security read of the launch code (#25, 2026-10-19), ruled later by the human. These are five breaches of CODING_STANDARDS.md ## Security in the data package. The package runs in the site's build and in the reader's browser. None is exploitable: the one attacker is a contributor's pull request, and the owner reviews and merges it. They share one concern and one kind of fix, so they are one issue.

    From the Security read of the launch code (#25, 2026-10-19), ruled later by the human. These are five breaches of CODING_STANDARDS.md ## Security in the data package. The package runs in the site's build and in the reader's browser. None is exploitable: the one attacker is a contributor's pull request, and the owner reviews and merges it. They share one concern and one kind of fix, so they are one issue.

    • C — `watch/state.json` is never validated.
    • Where: src/watch/state.ts:199-216 (data), and the site's src/lib/site.ts:262, which casts it to WatchState.
    • What: snapshot.retrieved_at reaches the site's <time datetime>. state.entries[entry.id] also finds inherited keys: an id of constructor gives last_read undefined.
    • Fix: a state schema (a date pattern on retrieved_at), checked in unreadSources, and lookups through Object.hasOwn.
    • D — `data/countries.json` has no schema.
    • Where: src/countries.ts:39, which casts it with as CountryVocabulary.
    • What: code, name, article, adjective and aliases are unchecked strings, and they reach option labels and the unread sentence.
    • Fix: a schema (code ^[A-Z]{2}$, length-capped strings), compiled inside validateDataset.
    • E — unbounded text.
    • Where: src/prose.ts:73, :76, and schema/ruleset.schema.json.
    • What: «\s*([^»]+?)\s*» backtracks polynomially over every dataset text. The schema caps only summary (maxLength 600): no other string has a maxLength, and no array a maxItems.
    • Fix: maxLength/maxItems across the schema; drop the \s*, and trim the capture.
    • F — unbounded depth.
    • Where: schema/ruleset.schema.json:934-939, and the walks at engine.ts:317 (evalCriterion), :840 (forEachCriterion), :265 (referencedFields), validate.ts:128 and prose.ts:183.
    • What: an any path's criteria refers back to criterion, so disjunctions nest without limit, and every walk recurses without a depth bound.
    • Fix: forbid any inside a path, or cap the depth in semanticErrors.
    • G — `data/exclusions.md` is unbounded and its twin has no schema.
    • Where: src/exclusions.ts:45-66.
    • What: the file is split and matched with no length cap, and the twin's route and limb ids are free text.
    • Fix: cap the length; hold the twin ids to the route and limb id patterns.

    Noted, not a breach: a history entry's source_url (schema:312, :540, :624) has no ^https:// pattern, unlike every live source_url. /dataset.json publishes it.

    Done when: each of C–G holds, each with a test that is red first. The work is a data-repository PR, then the site's data.lock moved past it.

    Folds in

    The kept-issue CLI's usage line names a path the steps that run it never use.

    src/watch/cli-kept-issue.ts's header shows node dist/src/watch/cli-kept-issue.js. Every step that keeps an issue runs the copy built on its own by npm run build:kept-issue, at dist/kept-issue-cli/src/watch/cli-kept-issue.js:

    • check.yml's report job;
    • the site's deploy report job (OytunOnal/permit-rulebook#122, PR OytunOnal/permit-rulebook#124), which builds only that copy from the commit data.lock pins.

    A reader following the header looks for a file the report jobs never build.

  4. #116 A builder's inner loop runs in seconds, and the slow suites run once, in the green

    What becomes possible: a builder's inner loop runs in seconds, and the slow browser and timer suites run once, in the green, with the per-file counts the green itself writes.

    What becomes possible: a builder's inner loop runs in seconds, and the slow browser and timer suites run once, in the green, with the per-file counts the green itself writes.

    Why now: the retros of #49, #104 and #110 (#97). The site's npm test takes 180–292 s and the data repository's npm run check 277–288 s, almost all of it in the browser and timer suites. In #110 the suite ran about 9 times in the foreground (~32 min), in #104 about 16 times (~76 min). Builders ran extra near-full runs only for per-suite counts, because vitest prints no per-file lines off a terminal. The load of parallel runs also made the s36 and s37 timing cases fail.

    The bet: the browser and timer suites in their own vitest project in each repository, a fast script for the inner loop, and a JSON report into .run/ from the green cut a slice's test time by more than half without losing a case. Spine may carry this for every project; then this issue closes into Spine's.

    Three parts, in both repositories:

    1. The slow suites in their own vitest project; a fast script (test:fast or the like) for the inner loop; the green still runs everything once.
    2. A JSON reporter beside the default one, writing per-file results into .run/, so the green yields the per-suite counts. vitest.config.ts's "about ten seconds" comment corrected.
    3. Two timing cases that fail under the load of parallel runs and pass alone stop depending on load, by an injected clock or by waiting on state instead of time: the data repository's s36 "does not let a body that unpacks slowly outlive the budget" (#104), and the site's s37 "a record grown by 2 after a live walk in another tab, then a reload" (#110). Folded in on the human's word, 2026-10-20 (#97).

  2. #150 One command removes a worktree: junction first, branches proven in their base

    From the retro of #133 (comment on #97, 2026-10-21), seen before in #110's; opened on the human's word.

    From the retro of #133 (comment on #97, 2026-10-21), seen before in #110's; opened on the human's word.

    What the session does by hand

    A site worktree holds node_modules\permit-rulebook-data, a junction to the sibling data checkout. Removing the worktree with the junction in place deletes the data repository's own files through it. So after every merge the session does, by hand: remove the junction alone (cmd //c rmdir "<wt>\node_modules\permit-rulebook-data"), recount the links, check that permit-rulebook-data/package.json still exists, remove the worktree, prove each branch it held is in its base, and delete those branches locally and on origin. Six times in #133.

    Done when

    • npm run worktree:remove -- <path> [<base>] does those steps in that order, and refuses while any junction or symbolic link under the worktree remains.
    • It checks that the data repository's package.json exists before and after, and stops if it does not.
    • It deletes only branches proven in <base> (default master), locally and on origin, and names each branch it kept and why.
    • Tested against a scratch worktree with a junction to a scratch target: the target survives, a branch not in its base is kept.

    Folds in

    From the retro of #151 (comment on #97, 2026-10-22), seen before in the retros of #110 and #133; folded on the human's word. The setup half: #151's build hand-wrote setup-151.mjs (a data worktree and a site worktree side by side, both installed, the data built, the site's file:../permit-rulebook-data link checked), used for nine tickets, the merge folder and the fix round, and a later check built two more pairs by hand.

    • npm run worktree:add -- <name> <base> [--detach] makes wt-<name> with a data worktree and a site worktree side by side, on a branch <base>-<name> from origin/<base> in each (or detached at it), installs both, builds the data, and checks that the site's permit-rulebook-data resolves to the data worktree beside it; it stops if the folder exists.
    • worktree:remove takes the same <name> and removes both worktrees and both branches by the steps above.
    • Tested with worktree:remove's scratch case: a pair added, then removed, the target data repository's package.json intact.

    Comments

    OytunOnal · 23 Oct 2026

    From the retro of #199 (2026-10-23, comment on #97), on the human's word: this issue stays at v1.2.7, with this slice's count for the next re-plan's WSJF.   5. #145 A kept issue reaches no one: it is opened by a bot and neither assigns nor mentions the owner

    Repository: OytunOnal/permit-rulebook-data (the kept-issue CLI), and the workflows that call it in both repositories.

    Repository: OytunOnal/permit-rulebook-data (the kept-issue CLI), and the workflows that call it in both repositories.

    Found by #125's real use (real use of #117), 2026-10-21; opened on the human's word.

    What the human sees

    Today three red runs that nobody watched kept their issues as #117 promised: #140 deploy run failed opened, data#40 check failed on master opened, data#31 watch run failed gained a comment. None of it reached the human. Their GitHub notifications for the day hold only GitHub Actions' own "… workflow run failed for master branch" entries (the ci_activity kind that #65 found "went out all ten days and changed nothing"), and nothing for #140, data#40 or data#31. The issues are opened and commented on by github-actions[bot], and a thread the human neither takes part in nor is mentioned in does not notify them.

    Why it matters

    #117 exists so that a red run nobody watches reaches someone. A kept issue that only waits to be found keeps #65's ten silent days possible.

    Done when

    • When the kept-issue CLI opens or reopens a kept issue, it assigns it to the repository's owner; each red comment @mentions the owner. The owner comes from the run's own context, never typed into the code.
    • A green run's closing line mentions no one.
    • Tested through the action the module decides and the exact gh argument lists it sends (ghArgumentsOf), as the kept-issue suites do today.
    • On the next real red, the human's notifications hold the kept issue itself (reason assign or mention), read by the session, not asked.

    #18 Permanent residence

    Planned in the ledger: wave 2 (v2).

    Planned in the ledger: wave 2 (v2).

    Permanent residence (v2 candidate, human's idea 2026-09-11: "permanent residency ve vatandaşlık yollarını da eklesek mi") · why: same reader, same journey, later step; and the rules are the shape this engine already handles — years, language level, income, no benefits · what it needs first, and it is not small: a history. Every PR rule keys on years of lawful residence under a particular status, and which status changes the count — study years often count half or not at all, a Blue Card shortens the track, Türkiye's Decision 1/80 runs on its own clock. Today the interview asks only where the reader stands now; PR turns a snapshot into a timeline, which is a new question set and a new engine concept · and it changes what a verdict is: work permits say "you meet this today", PR says "you will, on this path, in N years" — a projection, which is a much stronger claim than this product has ever made and a more harmful one to get wrong · bets A17 and A18: the timeline is worth building because the dated-and-quoted promise is worth more across a journey than across one step · gate: one country, one PR route, measured end to end before a second is opened.

    Carried from the ledger (`KANBAN.md`) on 2026-10-17, when the project moved onto the tracker (`docs/spine/migration-v2.md`).

    Comments

    OytunOnal · 17 Oct 2026

    Waits past the re-plan of 2026-10-17: it needs a residence history the interview does not ask, and #17 (v1.3.0) measures the appetite for it first (A16).

    #19 Citizenship

    Planned in the ledger: wave 2 (v2).

    Planned in the ledger: wave 2 (v2).

    Citizenship (v3 candidate, same source) · why: it is where the search volume is · why it is behind PR and not beside it: naturalisation is where nationality law bites hardest — dual-nationality rules that turn on the reader's other passport, renunciation requirements and their exceptions, discretionary integration judgements — and that class (data #8) already cost this project two slices and four country reads for one dimension. A large part of the surface would land in "quoted and dated · not scored", which is honest but is not the answer a person searching for it wants · bet A19: worth doing only after PR proves the timeline model, and only where a rule can be quoted rather than judged.

    #198 Each test file fits in 400 lines and each case in 40

    From #37's grill, ruling 14 (the human, 2026-10-23).

    From #37's grill, ruling 14 (the human, 2026-10-23).

    What a maintainer sees

    The lint #37 adds holds max-lines 400 and max-lines-per-function 40, counting every line. Since #149, a describe callback is held to it too, as each it and helper is. Ten test files are over 400 lines and no open slice touches them:

    • tests/s13.test.ts (908)
    • tests/first-paint.test.ts (754)
    • tests/s27.test.ts (544)
    • tests/s30.test.ts (511)
    • tests/s23.test.ts (484)
    • tests/s26.test.ts (464)
    • tests/s25.test.ts (443)
    • tests/orientation.test.ts (424)
    • tests/s19.test.ts (416)
    • tests/record.test.ts (407)

    The same applies to every it body or test helper over 40 lines in files no other issue takes, for example expectNothingLeft in tests/wire.ts (51). Until this lands, each one is a Suppression in eslint-suppressions.json, and each file over 400 lines a Ceiling in docs/spine/ceilings.json (#149).

    Done when

    • Each file above is at most 400 lines. It is split at a seam chosen for what each part checks, not cut to get under the number.
    • Every it and helper in them is at most 40 lines.
    • Case ids keep their names and outcomes across each split.
    • Their Ceilings are gone from docs/spine/ceilings.json, and their Suppressions pruned (npx eslint --prune-suppressions .).

    Reworded after #149 (grill ruling 1, 2026-10-24): the site's lint now runs on Spine's preset. A file over 400 lines is a Ceiling in docs/spine/ceilings.json, held to its count until it is within 400. Every other existing breach is a Suppression in eslint-suppressions.json, whose count per file and rule cannot grow. A describe callback is no longer exempt from the function limit. The dated exception list and tests/lint.test.ts are gone, so the lines above name what replaced them.

    #222 The analytics beacon runs as a pinned copy with its integrity hash

    What becomes possible: the analytics beacon follows Spine's general rule "A third-party script is pinned to a version and loaded with its integrity hash" (SPINE_STANDARDS.md, Security), so the product needs no departure from it, and a change on Cloudflare's side cannot change the script our pages run.

    What becomes possible: the analytics beacon follows Spine's general rule "A third-party script is pinned to a version and loaded with its integrity hash" (SPINE_STANDARDS.md, Security), so the product needs no departure from it, and a change on Cloudflare's side cannot change the script our pages run.

    Why now: PR #219 moved this product's general rules into Spine's SPINE_STANDARDS.md. Today the beacon is the one exception: src/lib/site.ts loads https://static.cloudflareinsights.com/beacon.min.js, unversioned and with no integrity hash, and the Content-Security-Policy allows it by name. The human ruled to follow the rule wherever the product can, rather than write a departure (2026-10-24): "kurallara uyabildiğin noktada uy. gereksiz departure ekleme".

    The bet: Cloudflare Web Analytics counts a page view sent by a pinned copy of its beacon, served from our own origin with its integrity hash, just as it counts one sent by the script it serves. If it does not, the departure comes back with that evidence.

    What to build

    • The beacon is a copy of a named version, committed or carried at build time, loaded with its integrity hash.
    • The Content-Security-Policy no longer allows static.cloudflareinsights.com. It still allows the reports to cloudflareinsights.com/cdn-cgi/rum.
    • CODING_STANDARDS.md's Content-Security-Policy line names what the pages run after the change.

    Done when

    • A page view on the live site shows in Cloudflare Web Analytics with the pinned copy. That needs the human's dashboard.
    • A test proves every script the pages load is our own, or pinned with its hash.

#197 Two checks on a real device: the phone pass and a screen reader on the results

open for 1 day

From the v1.2.2 gate critique (docs/spine/critiques/2026-10-23-v1.2.2-gate-critique.md, "For the human"). Its headless walk could not check these.

Why it is yours: both need a real device, a phone with its on-screen keyboard and a screen reader, which this session's headless Chrome does not have.

1. A real phone, one pass

On https://permitrulebook.com, on your phone:

  1. Tap the passport field ("Search the list") on question 1 or wherever it comes. Type "Tür" with the on-screen keyboard up.
  2. Pass: the list shows "Türkiye" above the keyboard, and tapping it commits Türkiye.
  3. Fail: the list is hidden under the keyboard, or a tap commits another row.
  4. Open the header's Menu.
  5. Pass: the menu opens, and the page under it does not jump when it closes.
  6. Finish a walk with a job offer in the Netherlands and open the first card.
  7. Pass: you can reach the next card ("within reach") with your thumb without scrolling past more than about a screen of quotes.
  8. Fail: two screens or more of quotes. That is #191, so add your count there.

2. A screen reader on the results screen

With VoiceOver (iPhone) or TalkBack (Android), on a finished walk's results:

  1. Swipe through the top of the results.
  2. Pass: the strip's counters are read in their order with their numbers, for example "4 open", "23 not yet".
  3. Reach a folded country section.
  4. Pass: it is announced as collapsed with its line, and opens on a double tap.
  5. On /feedback/, use the copy button.
  6. Pass: "Copied" is announced.

What to do

Comment here, one line per check: "pass", or what you heard or saw instead. Where a check fails, I open a bug from your line.

#206 Delete the Cloudflare Pages preview project

open for 1 day

Why it is yours: the Cloudflare dashboard needs your Cloudflare login, and no tool here can sign in to it.

What and why

The per-branch preview on Cloudflare Pages is no longer used. #199's fix round deletes its setup doc (docs/spine/preview.md) and its build script (scripts/preview-build.sh), on your ruling of 2026-10-23 (comment on #199). Once the script is gone, every branch push would make the Cloudflare project fail its build, so the project goes too.

Steps

  1. Open https://dash.cloudflare.com → Compute (Workers & Pages).
  2. Look for a Pages project named `permit-rulebook`. It is connected to Git for OytunOnal/permit-rulebook, its production branch is preview-only, and its build command is bash scripts/preview-build.sh.
  3. Open it → Settings → at the bottom, Delete project, and confirm with its name.
  4. Optional: at https://github.com/settings/installations, the Cloudflare Workers and Pages app. If OytunOnal/permit-rulebook was its only repository, uninstall it; otherwise remove that repository from its list.

Pass

The Workers & Pages list has no permit-rulebook project. https://permit-rulebook.pages.dev no longer serves the site; Cloudflare answers with its own not-found page.

If it fails

  • No such project: it was never set up, or was already removed. Comment "no project" and close this issue.
  • Delete is refused, for example because a custom domain is attached: comment with Cloudflare's message, and leave this open. permitrulebook.com itself is served by GitHub Pages and must not be detached from there.

Note: from this machine permit-rulebook.pages.dev resolves to ::1 and an address that times out, so the project's state could not be checked from here.

docs/spine/ARCHITECTURE.md

ARCHITECTURE — Permit Rulebook (as of v1, public and announced 2026-09-09)

Updated at every slice exit. Three diagrams, one question each: where the facts come from, how they become a site, where the reader's answers stay. Labels are names; what each name means is in the list below the diagrams.

1 · Data flow — where the facts come from

flowchart LR
    Sources -->|fetch, hash| Watch --> Flags
    Flags -.->|a person edits| Dataset
    Dataset --> Gates
    Exclusions --> Gates

2 · Pipeline — how the facts become a site

flowchart LR
    Lock -->|which data commit| Build
    Dataset --> Build
    Engine --> Build
    Tokens --> Build
    Build --> Dist --> CI --> Pages
    Watch -->|dataset-updated| CI

3 · Trust boundary — where the answers stay

flowchart LR
    subgraph web["permitrulebook.com"]
        Pages
    end
    subgraph device["The reader's device"]
        RoutePage["Route page"]
        Interview
        Record
    end
    Pages --> RoutePage
    Pages --> Interview
    RoutePage -->|"check yours"| Interview
    Interview --> Record
    Interview -.->|"report a wrong value"| Tracker

Nothing crosses the boundary from the device outward except a reader's own click on the tracker link. Answers never leave the device.

The names

Diagram 1 — permit-rulebook-data (sibling repository; data CC BY 4.0, code MIT)

  • Sources — the official pages and PDFs: ZAV, BOE, UGE, IND, service-public.gouv.fr, europa.eu, efta.int, buzer.
  • Watch — the daily re-read (src/watch, GitHub Actions): strategies html, pdf-text (embedded-font text layer, bytes hash beside words hash) and link liveness; slices bind each page to its operative text; glyph corrections are declared data; human_tier: 2 since 2026-09-10 (two sources a browser reads and this fetcher cannot — see the glossary); runs daily in GitHub Actions and, after its state is pushed, files an issue (source-change) per flag this run created or changed; a flag still open on the second UTC morning after it was raised turns the run red and is named in one issue the run keeps, overdue flags (#62). A red run on master keeps watch run failed, and the first green one closes it (#117). Every kept issue is decided in one module, kept-issue, and carried to the tracker by one CLI, built on its own so a broken build cannot silence it, that runs gh with no shell (#117). Two readers, a fetcher and a browser (Chrome over its own protocol), and the coverage gate ask one module, trust-line, where the watch may go: the entry address, a redirect target, every address a name resolves to (the fetcher's; a name Chrome resolves for itself is the stated gap) and the page a reader lands on. It imports only the failure wording; the fetcher keeps only HTTP (#104). What a finished run says (its flags, its log lines and its exit code) is decided in one module, run-says; the CLI only reads, prints and writes (#72). It audits every installed dependency first; a known advisory at high or above turns the run red at its end and stops none of its reading (#24).
  • Flags — one file per change, with the page's excerpt in a fenced code block so nothing in it acts on the issue, and every line around it ours (#72); walked by a person, who edits the dataset or records what was found, within two days.
  • Dataset — data/dataset.json: 29 routes in 4 countries, 23 of them asked about and 6 quoted only; every numeric value with its source URL, verbatim quote, retrieval date and append-only history; per route its statements (preconditions and caveats in the source's words), its readings (ours, declared) and a scope value with the limbs it does not ask. A question whose options are rungs of one amount is marked ordered; a criterion with paths and each path carry a label; a question names a route only through a {route:<id>} slot, filled with the route's what_it_is (#151). A path answer someone else arranges (a transfer, a hosting agreement) is marked is_arranged, and only the path question may carry the mark (schema 0.8.3, #167). Beside it data/countries.json (199 passport issuers, labels, aliases, the article a name takes; classes.eu_eea_ch is a rule with four sourced legs; and 11 territories, none a row, each with the passport its people hold and an official sentence that says so) and schema/ruleset.schema.json, the public contract.
  • Reader's words from the data — the data package composes the words the site prints from the dataset: a country's name with its article, at a sentence's start and as a possessive; the scope line in two forms (where the reader has answered, "checked against your answers"; where not, "the checker asks about it"); a {place} filled "in <country>" or "in the country you move to"; and which kind of host a source is on, authority or consolidated text (#151). The site holds the labels; the package holds the facts.
  • Exclusions — data/exclusions.md: what was researched and left out, by route, as prose plus a fenced machine twin.
  • Gates — npm run check: schema and semantic validation · watch coverage both ways · quote fidelity (every shipped quote found in its snapshot) · prose provenance (every renderable sentence is authority, ours or label, keyed on the declared kind) · every route and notice source, and every learn link, on an https host on the authority list or the consolidated-text list · labels on paths · words on an ordered range · route names only in slots · each territory's passport on the list with an https source (#151) · the arranged mark only on the path question (#167). A failing gate fails the build downstream. The data repository runs them on every push to master, every pull request and after every watch run (check.yml); a red check after a watch run keeps check failed on master, and the next green one closes it (#117).

Diagram 2 — permit-rulebook (this repository; MIT)

  • Engine — pure functions in the data package: eligibility by eq/in/gte/points/any, band edges = thresholds, deciding path, still-reachable, points as the best row an answer satisfies, questions by information gain, provable single-step unlocks, notices; property tests over thousands of generated profiles. A disjunction is judged path by path in its own module (src/disjunction.ts): it is a hard fail only when no path is open, every open path asks its questions, and when every path failed the deciding path is the nearest gap or else the open path with the fewest unmet rules. reckonAskingRoutes names, from the same walk as the asking rule, the routes a question is asked for and the open ones; questions-do-work checks, per destination and per rung, that an answer moves some route's decision (#133). isStepGated decides which routes wait on a step; routesAnOfferFrees counts, per country, the routes a job offer would free for a reader with none; a gap is said once, exact and with its period, and a band open at the bottom names the line it is under (#167).
  • Tokens — tokens.css (the design system) and identity.css (the identity pair: the tilted PR stamp over the read-date stamp; one source, inlined by the route page, linked by the interview).
  • Build — Astro, base derived from SITE_URL, the site's address, which is one literal line in pages.yml: the deploy reads no repository variable (#110). An invalid dataset breaks the build; the social card is rendered from the dataset's own facts with a sidecar that makes a stale card fail CI.
  • Dist — 38 pages (the interview, /data/, /feedback/, the status page, the 404, 4 country pages, 29 route pages generated from the dataset, never typed) plus 29 route JSON endpoints, the dataset and the countries as JSON, the sitemap, favicons, the social card.
  • CI — pages.yml: both repositories checked out, data gates, build, tests with a real browser over dist/, the lint, asset truth, base-path check, tap targets at 390 px, the carry, then deploy. The lint (eslint.config.mjs) loads Spine's preset from spine-lint/, which holds the limits, 400 lines a file and 40 a function with every line counted. The files over 400 lines are Ceilings in docs/spine/ceilings.json, each held to its count until within 400; every other existing breach is a Suppression in eslint-suppressions.json, whose count per file and rule cannot grow. The issues that pay them off carry the deadline on their milestones (#149). The carry (scripts/carry-assets) copies the live generation's assets into dist/, only from the site's own address and only bytes the previous build's digest list attests; it is a deep module whose entry, the flow, is given the live site, the build directory and the clock as ports. Its domain parts are private (generation/, outcome/, the ceilings in live-deploy/); its adapters and its log wording sit beside the command that wires them, and it never fails the deploy (#110, #199). Beside the build, the audit job reads the site's lockfile: an advisory reddens the run, never holds the deploy; only report waits on it, and runs whatever its result. report runs last, on a scheduled or dispatched run's first attempt, after the build, the deploy, the pin and the audit: a red keeps one issue, deploy run failed, and the first green closes it, through the data package's kept-issue CLI built from the commit data.lock names (#117). Both workflows run on a named image, ubuntu-26.04, never -latest (#24).
  • Pages — GitHub Pages at the custom domain, HTTPS enforced; static files only; the accepted cost is no response headers of our own.

Diagram 3 — the reader

  • Route page — rules in interview order, the rail with its label list, quotes with language and read date, the scope block, the data door (JSON, repository, tracker), one call to action that pre-scopes the interview. It dates only what it prints: each headline figure carries its own read date, and the stamp, the country cards and the liveness line read one set, the quotes the page prints. A site-wide page (/data/, /feedback/, the 404, the sitemap) dates the whole dataset, as the interview does (#76).
  • Interview — questions by information gain, then the verdict; runs entirely in the browser. The page is wiring alone: it builds the browser's history adapter and the record's store and starts the interview, one deep module, src/lib/interview/. Every decision a gesture leads to (the screen, its lines, how it meets the reader, the beat, the save, the history move) comes from one pure step, interviewAfter; the drawing parts only apply it. The browser's history is a deep module of its own behind a port, interview-history, so Back across two tabs is tested in Node; Back names where it goes and never the screen it is on. The passport search is a module whose controller holds no DOM, country-search, and an arrival is one call, arrivalOf (#208). The verdict is one deep module, src/lib/verdict/ (#167): verdictOf decides everything the results screen says (the headline and masthead, the groups and their order, each card with its rail and gap, the steps, the offer line, the arranged line, the folds) and verdictHtml draws it, with its own stylesheet; the interview only paints, announces and records one history entry per verdict. Steps are only what the reader can do; what a job offer would open and what someone else arranges are said beside them, never counted as steps. Every word a screen prints lives in src/lib/copy/, one file per screen or part, read by name. Its first paint is one module, src/lib/first-paint.ts (#66). An inline head script reads the record, the link and the width before anything paints, and writes a cover and the first-paint seeds on <html>. The stylesheet it renders hides what each cover hides. The interview takes the covers off once, as it draws the reader's first screen, so no arrival moves the page. The masthead has one writer per screen: a question screen's is questionMastheadOf, beside the question card; a results or notice screen's is composed inside the verdict module and carried by the screen as data, so no question screen can carry a claim about a comparison (#172). Its own markup is one emphasis, and every other word, the dataset's included, is escaped (#25). A question card a gesture draws comes with a beat: it fades in, and for --motion-beat (tokens.css) every commit of an answer, click or Enter, is refused in one place, motion or none. The first paint and the verdict have none (#86). A question fewer than all the open routes read carries its asked-for line, drawn from reckonAskingRoutes: the routes by name and country, or counted per country past two. A pencil change of answer replays the interview, and the answers no route reads any more are named once as set aside in the declared panel (its folded summary on a phone); a money row answered "Doesn't apply…" keeps its pencil (#133). Every screen but /data/ speaks the reader's words, not the model's: no "scored", "rule set" or "this interview" (GLOSSARY, Scope statement). A link to a source says whose page it is ("Official page", or "Consolidated text on buzer.de"); an abbreviation is explained the first time each page uses it (src/lib/gloss.ts, results cards included); a criterion with paths is headed by its label with one line per path; the passport search answers a territory with the passport its people hold. One walk over every page the build draws (tests/reader-pages.ts) holds these (#151).
  • Record — localStorage (legacy key migrated) or nothing; a print stylesheet for a paper copy. A record longer than any the page writes (8 KB) is no record, and is never parsed (#25). Start over clears it on every address: a shared device keeps nothing of a reader (#208).
  • Tracker — permit-rulebook-data/issues with bug, design-flaw, new-need; reached only by the reader's own click.

Not yet real-green (s6): the phone walk on the live site, the isolated v1-gate critique's blockers cleared, and the announcement.
